The clone was supposed to be free, but a clone diverges as the live database is written, so every run paid real blocks for a file every byte of which the sidecar re-derives | Restore the `EVENTS_DB` backup and prune calls in `bin/realtest.sh` and flip `bin/test-realtest.sh`'s assertion back to requiring a store copy |
## Sidecar shutdown and the boot log flood (2026-09-13)

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | What bound does the shutdown log drain get? | 5s (`logging.DefaultShutdownDrain`) | Measured from the log's own records: a healthy teardown puts 90µs between the `shutdown` record and the `exit` record, and the replacement process started 34ms later. 5s is a large multiple of every healthy teardown observed and a small fraction of launchd's 20s exit timeout, so a stuck drain costs a bounded pause rather than a hung service | Change the constant in `internal/logging/logging.go`; `Close` (unbounded) still exists for tests that own both ends of the forwarder |
| 2026-09-13 | Is the catch-up boundary per item (the existing idiom's mtime test) or a process-wide phase? | A process-wide phase for these six operations | The six span four packages and none of them holds the item's own clock; threading an mtime into `convert` and `tail` to reach the same answer would put the classification in the wrong layer. The existing per-item idiom in `held.go`/`stale.go` is untouched and still governs the conclusions it already levels | Delete `catchupOperations` and the `BeginCatchup`/`EndCatchup` pair; each site then classifies its own item |
| 2026-09-13 | When does the window close? | At the end of the first poll pass that walked EVERY watcher | That pass IS the boot walk: the cycle's first rescan discovers and rewinds the corpus, and the first pass that runs all the way through has picked up and converted it. An abandoned pass has not, so it does not close the window | Close the window on a tick count or on `beginCycle` returning instead of on `drainedPass` |
| 2026-09-13 | Six integration subjects asserted a per-item record the window now levels. Amend them, or amend the window? | Neither weakens: five now read the log at `AGENT_REPL_LOG_LEVEL=debug`, where the invariant promises the detail still is, and one (a steady-state subject that was racing the boot walk) waits on the new `catchup-end` edge instead of the first production cycle | The subjects assert per-item detail, which is exactly what DEBUG carries during catch-up; the steady-state one was asserting a condition that was not yet true when it looked, which is a synchronization defect in the subject rather than a leveling question | Drop the `ExtraEnv` lines and `awaitCatchupEnd`; the subjects then need the window gone |
| 2026-09-13 | The 6.28 GB `shim-claude-sidecar.err.log` | ALREADY FIXED, no code change; a regression test added | Commit `0366f212d` made both services durable-only (`terminalEmergencyOnly`), and the file has not grown since 2026-09-11 17:15, when the disk it filled made the process panic. The content is the service's own structured JSON record stream mirrored to launchd's `StandardErrorPath`, ending in that panic and its goroutine dump. `openLoggerTo` now names the terminal so a test can prove a normal boot writes nothing there | Not applicable |
| 2026-09-13 | 369 `store.db.slow-query` warns, 15 of them in one hour after the `lock_wait_ms` split, every one `lock_wait_ms=0`. Is an over-budget wall clock a defect report at all? | RE-LEVELED with a measurement: a single over-budget sample is `info`, and `warn` is kept for a statement FAMILY persistently over budget (8 of its last 16) | `duration_ms` is wall clock, and modernc's SQLite runs in-process on the calling goroutine, so a descheduled goroutine and a slow statement are the same number. The same batch shapes run against a byte-for-byte copy of the same 1.65 GB database took 1-2ms (400 batches, six concurrent readers, 113 MB WAL, worst case 15ms), so nothing about the statements, the indexes, the database size or the WAL explains 1595ms for 7 rows. A lost index or a reintroduced scan is a property of the statement and fills the family window; host contention takes one unlucky statement. No threshold was loosened and no observation was dropped | Leave every sample at `warn` and accept ~15 unactionable warns a day, or move the budget off the total onto a phase the store cannot separate |
| 2026-09-13 | Two `store.db.open-page` ERRORs, "begin read transaction: database is locked (5) (SQLITE_BUSY)" | FIXED at the source: `OpenPage`, `ReadPage` and `BashRun` open with `ReadOnly: true`, so a pure read takes only its WAL snapshot | `_txlock=immediate` is a property of the CONNECTION, so the write path's requirement reached reads that write nothing and made a page repaint queue for — and be refused by — the write lock, which is the failure WAL is chosen to remove. A deferred read in WAL still fixes one snapshot at its first statement, so the watch pin and its page still agree | Retry the read on SQLITE_BUSY, which would paper over the contention rather than remove it |
| 2026-09-13 | One `store.db.live-work` ERROR, "scanning live agents: context canceled" | RE-LEVELED to `info` as an abandoned call; the error still reaches the server unchanged | The caller hung up mid-statement. Nothing about the database failed and no operator action exists, and a healthy store writing error records during ordinary operation is how an error log stops being read — the same reasoning already applied to a stale pointer | Leave it at error, or swallow it entirely (which would lose the record that the call arrived at all) |

## Realtest leftovers and the gap between sweeps (2026-09-13)

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | A leftover registry row is removed how — by deleting the row from `wsm.db`, or through the daemon? | Through the daemon: `close`, then `forget`, written into the command-file ingress and waited for in the registry | The registry is the daemon's, and `forget` has no rpc arm at all, so the command file is the only door. A harness that edited the database would be repairing the evidence rather than exercising the product's own removal path — the one the owner would use — and a product path that does not undo what it does would stay hidden | Have `CleanLeftovers` issue SQL instead; the snapshot read in `state.go` would then need a write path it deliberately does not have |
| 2026-09-13 | The end-of-test leftover assertion FAILS the realtest, where every other cleanup in that file only reports. Which rule wins? | It fails | The report-only rule is about residues the PRODUCT cannot remove (a repository record no verb forgets). A registry row this run created and could have removed is not one: it is the run breaking its own contract with the owner, and a green run that left one is a green run that lied | Turn the `t.Errorf` in `wsActAssertNoLeftovers` into a `t.Logf`; the sweep-end check in `bin/realtest.sh` would then be the only thing failing on it |
| 2026-09-13 | Where does the sweep's end-of-run cleanup live — after the run loop, or in a trap? | An EXIT trap, guarded on `BASHPID` and on the sweep having started | "The run left the registry as it found it" has to hold when a realtest failed, when `go test` panicked and when the operator interrupted the sweep. A block after the loop is exactly the code that does not run on the paths where the cleanup matters most | Move the body of `sweep_end` back inline before the verdict |
| 2026-09-13 | The between-sweeps high-water mark: a timestamp, or a snapshot? | A snapshot — the inode-keyed `Snapshot`, plus both Emacs buffer sizes — beside the timestamp | Two source classes carry no timestamps at all: the services' `.err.log` and the two Emacs buffers. A time alone could only ever report them whole, every sweep, forever, and a scan that cried wolf every run would be turned off within a week | Drop the `Snapshot` and the sizes from `SweepMark`; the scan then needs an allowlist, which the harvest bar forbids |
| 2026-09-13 | Every line of `*Warnings*` is a finding, with no pattern set. Is that too wide? | It stands | `*Messages*` is a mixed buffer where a severity shape separates a problem from an echo, which is why `messages.go` carries patterns. Nothing but `display-warning` writes to `*Warnings*`, so a pattern set there could only ever drop warnings whose wording nobody anticipated — and the harvest has no allowlist | Give `HarvestWarningsBuffer` the `messagePatterns` treatment |
| 2026-09-13 | The three harness checks (leftover report, gap scan, leftover clean and mark) run under `bin/suite-slot.sh` or not? | Not | The slot exists because every SUITE here is sized to fill the machine. These are single-process reads — a snapshot of `wsm.db`, a walk of the log files, two read-only probes — that start no editor and run nothing in parallel. Holding a machine-wide slot for one would make a sweep wait on somebody else's vitest run to find out whether the owner's registry is clean | Wrap `run_harness_check` in `suite-slot.sh`; `bin/test-realtest.sh`'s sequencing cases then need the slot marker's new lines |
| 2026-09-13 | The gap scan's report collides with realtest 1's `MANIFEST.md` in the run directory. Where does it go? | `between-sweeps/MANIFEST.md`, its own subdirectory | The spec's own rule for every realtest after the first, for the same reason: realtest 1 predates the rule and writes the run directory itself, so anything else writing there is overwritten by whichever ran second | Write the section into each realtest's manifest instead; every one of them would then carry the same copy |


## Log sweep, sidecar (2026-09-13)

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | 80 `api-error` WARN records, all of them the vendor's own recorded mid-turn failure (45 ENOTFOUND, 25 ECONNRESET, 10 DNS) — fix or re-level? | Re-leveled to INFO in `internal/convert/system.go`; not to debug | An OBSERVED fault is not an OWNED one. The request was the vendor's, the failure was recorded by the vendor's own transcript writer, and every step this converter takes succeeds: the line is read, the kind is mapped onto the taxonomy, and it lands on `AgentUpdate.api_error` as the page line a reader sees. Nothing is degraded and nothing is refused, which is what the sidecar AGENTS.md reserves warn for. It stays at INFO rather than debug because it fires only on a real vendor failure and an operator correlating a stalled turn with the network wants it in a default-level log | Restore `at.ctxWarn("api-error")` |
| 2026-09-13 | 38 `discover-meta` ERROR records, every one of them ENFILE ("too many open files in system") on a meta read — sidecar defect, or the host's? | Both answered separately: the misclassification is fixed here (its own hold reason, WARN, message stating the retry), the fd exhaustion is reported as a host condition and NOT chased in the sidecar | The errno is ENFILE, not EMFILE: the SYSTEM had no descriptor left, not this process. Discovery reads one meta at a time, sequentially, and `tail`/`discover` open and close per read holding no fd between them — so the sidecar is not what exhausted them. What IS the sidecar's is the classification: the branch's own justification for shouting ("unlike a missing file this one will not fix itself") is false for a deferred read, which the very next rescan retries and which clears on its own | Delete `transientReadFailure` and `holdMetaReadDeferred`; every read failure then routes to `meta_unreadable` at error again |
| 2026-09-13 | Should the deferred-read hold drop to info or debug instead of warn? | It stays WARN | The transcript IS held and its records ARE unattributed while it lasts, which is degraded-but-handled — what the sidecar AGENTS.md reserves warn for — and a box running out of descriptors is something an operator should see. Only the ERROR claim of an owned, permanent fault was wrong | Pass `"info"` to `holdTranscript` for the deferred reason |
| 2026-09-13 | 5 `task-stop` WARNs, "names an agent task no launch on this stream opened" — 23f26d5e2 deliberately kept this arm at warn as "a pointier expected-but-absent launch signal". Overrule it? | Split the arm rather than overrule it: warn survives for a converter that read the file from byte 0, debug for one that resumed mid-file | The earlier ruling is right whenever the launch COULD have been seen, and that is exactly what the converter never checked. All five records sit at offset ~50 MB of a transcript a restarted reader had joined minutes earlier, stopping agents whose launches a previous process had already converted and committed. The pointier signal is kept where it is actually pointy | Delete `Converter.joined`/`joinedOffset` and `resumedMidFile`; the arm then warns unconditionally again |

## Daemon log sweep (2026-09-13)

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | `daemon.workspace.bring_up` records "the session is parked behind a cold gate" at WARN, 31 times in the current logs. Fix or re-level? | RE-LEVELED to info | The gate is a designed product state, not a condition to remediate: `startSession`'s own doc says a nil `SessionStarted` with a nil error "is an answer and not a failure", `raiseColdGate` publishes the cost to the footer and the feed, and the user answers pay, clear or compact. Nothing failed and nothing is to be fixed, so it cannot stand in a log read for defects. The record is kept, not deleted -- it is the only one carrying `context_tokens` and `requested_model` -- and the sibling record the caller writes for the same event was already info | Restore `log.Warn` in `Fleet.startSession`; `TestTheColdGateIsRecordedAtInfo` then fails and names the choice |
| 2026-09-13 | Three daemon lifecycle records stand at WARN for outcomes nothing went wrong in: `daemon.cmd.claim` losing the boot claim (4), `daemon.boot.close_missing_dir` closing a vanished directory (3), `daemon.shimclient.standdown` sweeping an in-flight spawn at shutdown (3). Fix or re-level? | RE-LEVELED to info, all three | Each is a mechanism succeeding, not a condition to remediate. Emacs spawns a daemon whenever it cannot tell one is serving, so a claim loser is the exclusivity ruling working and it exits having written nothing. The automatic close of a workspace whose directory is gone is the OWNER'S OWN RULING of 2026-09-11, and a ruled action that succeeded states what it did. The stand-down sweep exists precisely to catch a spawn in flight when the shutdown lands, so catching one is the sweep succeeding. In all three cases the arms beside them stay exactly as loud: a stale-addr claim refusal, a stat that failed for any reason but absence, a close that could not be written, and a kill that failed | Restore `log.Warn` at the three sites; `TestTheClaimLoserRecordsAtInfo`, `TestTheRuledAutomaticCloseIsRecordedAtInfo` and `TestTheSweptSpawnIsRecordedAtInfo` then fail and name the choice |

## Sidecar LOST sweep (2026-09-13)

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | 4 `lost-policy` and 3 `bash-lost` WARNs reading `went_silent`. An earlier sweep called them "genuine, level correct", which is not an outcome. Reader defect, or ordinary lifecycle? | READER DEFECT for 3 of the 4; fixed rather than re-leveled. The 4th is a genuine LOST and its WARN stands | The named spools were checked. `b3bkckml9`, `bc50109ws` and `beabw25qa` each END on `[exited with code 0]`, at exactly the byte offset the reader's own record reports — so it read the terminator and did not recognize it. This reader knew only the `EXIT=` line OUR harness scripts write; the bracket line is written by the vendor's own background-shell wrapper and so is the only terminator a run no script of ours wrapped ever gets. Over that session's 67 shell spools, 32 end on the bracket and all 11 carrying `EXIT=` carry the bracket AFTER it, which also defeated the old "the marker is the batch's last line" rule outright. `bp5nba0f0` carries no terminator of either spelling and genuinely went silent, so it keeps its warn: that arm is the policy working | Delete `wrapperExitPrefix`, `parseWrapperExit` and the `finalLine`/`lastLineOf` split in `internal/handler/shell.go`; the nine new subjects in `shell_test.go` then fail and name the choice |

## Owner rulings: six look-and-feel asides noticed during the playtests (2026-09-13, owner)

These are OWNER-ORDERED visual changes, the one path the no-look-and-feel
rule allows.

1. Emacs tab bar: exactly one space between `[N]` and the workspace name.
   Never `[3]    test-workspace`.
2. Webapp sidebar: remove the small white dot preceding each workspace
   entry; the larger status dot already carries the meaning.
3. Webapp feed: the left-hand "nothing held" section. The owner wants it
   DIAGNOSED and discussed first: what it shows when something IS held;
   the empty state should say nothing rather than announce emptiness.
4. Emacs tab bar: when selected, only `<workspace-name>` is underlined,
   never `[N] `, never a leading or trailing space.
5. Emacs tab bar background, DIAGNOSE BEFORE FIXING and define the terms
   in AGENTS.md: "full" = the whole `[N] <workspace-name>` entry carries
   the status color; "partial" = only `[N]` carries it. Rule: full IF AND
   ONLY IF the workspace's agent-repl panels (webapp and input window) are
   open; partial IF AND ONLY IF they are not.
6. Opening a workspace by any means other than switching to an already
   open one (creation, re-open, register, fork) opens its agent-repl
   panels immediately, as `SPC o c` does, before it is switched to.

## Panels open when a workspace opens (owner ruling #6), 2026-09-13

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | Where the one hook goes, given six ways to open a workspace | `agent-repl-roster--open-tab` (roster.el), the single editor-side birth every open already funnels through — create, fork, one-shot, register, re-open and any daemon-side arrival | Verbs learn the identity at different moments and one-shot never selects at all; the tab is the one event all six share, and it is strictly before the landing the verbs perform | Drop the `agent-repl--panels-open-on-arrival` call from `--open-tab` |
| 2026-09-13 | How "already open when Emacs started" is told apart from a later arrival, since both are roster rows | The FIRST reconcile of the session arms the gate (`agent-repl--panels-arrivals-armed`) and opens nothing; every arrival after it opens | The startup roster IS the set of workspaces that were open when Emacs started, and the startup path already governs their panels | Set `agent-repl--panels-arrivals-armed` to `t` at load |
| 2026-09-13 | How the reason reaches the roster, which does not know which verb asked | Each verb records `created`/`forked`/`reopened`/`registered` against the minted ref id and the arrival CONSUMES it; an unclaimed arrival reads `arrived` | The reason is only knowable at the verb, and a table keyed by the daemon's own id cannot mis-attribute it | Log a constant reason and delete `agent-repl--panels-arrival-reasons` |
| 2026-09-13 | A one-shot is fire-and-forget and never selects, so it has no landing to hang the open on | It claims its arrival at the create ACK, independent of `select`, and reads `created` | The ruling names one-shot explicitly; not selecting is about where the user stands, not about whether the workspace has panels | Move the claim inside the `(when select ...)` branch |
| 2026-09-13 | The panels must be built for a workspace that is NOT current yet | The open runs inside `agent-repl--call-in-background-workspace`, the existing anchor the webview mount and pre-creation already use | Anchoring is what keeps a background workspace's panels out of the looked-at workspace's frame; the caller's perspective, window and buffer are restored afterwards | Call `agent-repl--panels-show-or-open` directly |
## Tab-bar owner rulings 1, 4 and 5 (2026-09-13)

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | Ruling 1 asks for exactly one space between `[N]` and the name. Three things could each add one — the padding format's leading space, the badge run's own trailing space, a name carrying leading whitespace. Normalize one of them, or all three? | ALL THREE, in the renderer, so the gap cannot be widened from anywhere. `agent-repl--render-tab` emits the single space itself, strips the padding format's leading whitespace, trims the name, and drops a badge run that is blank; `agent-repl--tab-badge-str` no longer joins blank parts | The gap was the sum of independent contributors, so fixing the loudest one leaves the shape reachable by the others. A roster row whose priority label is present but empty joined into a run made of nothing but spaces, which the renderer then padded on BOTH sides and met the padding's leading space — three spaces, which is the shape the owner reported. The padding format keeps its TRAILING half, which is a real width fill and unchanged on screen | Restore `(propertize (format agent-repl-tab-name-padding name) ...)` and the `(concat " " img-str " ")` badge wrap in `agent-repl--render-tab`; the six gap tests in `test-status.el` then fail and name the ruling |
| 2026-09-13 | Ruling 4 wants the underline under the name alone. The renderer put `:underline t` on the separator, the bracket AND the name, and the padded name run carried the gap and the fill inside the underlined string. Face-level change, or a run-level split? | RUN-LEVEL SPLIT. `:underline t` is gone from `separator-face` and `bracket-face`, and the name run is now three runs — an un-underlined gap space, the underlined name, an un-underlined width fill — so the marked range is exactly the name's characters | Dropping the underline from the two faces alone would still have underlined the padding spaces inside the name run, which is precisely the "never a leading or trailing space" half of the ruling. Splitting the runs makes the extent structural rather than a property of how the padding format happens to be written | Put `,@(when underline (list :underline t))` back on `separator-face` and `bracket-face` and give the gap and fill runs `name-face*`; the seven underline-extent tests in `test-status.el` then fail |
| 2026-09-13 | Ruling 5 asked for a DIAGNOSIS first: is the full/partial background keyed on the panels-open fact? | CLEAN DEFECT ON TWO COUNTS, both fixed. `agent-repl--ws-display-state` did key on `agent-repl--ws-agent-open-p`, but (a) it layered a SECOND suppression on top — a local ready-view dwell latch that dropped a `:ready` workspace to partial two seconds after the user stood in it, with its panels open — and (b) the panels-open predicate itself accepted the webapp panel ALONE, so a layout with the composer dismissed drew full. The latch, its dwell clock, its transition-clear and its `agent-repl-ready-view-fade-delay` knob are deleted; the predicate now requires both panels | The rule is an IF AND ONLY IF, so any second input to the decision breaks it in one direction and a weaker predicate breaks it in the other. The fade was an implementer's presentation modifier, never an owner ruling — nothing in the changelog or this file holds it — and it made the extent say something about attention rather than about panels. The heartbeat it rode on survives as the repaint clock it always also was | Restore the ready-view block above `agent-repl--ws-display-state` and its `:ready`/acknowledged cond arm, and make `agent-repl--wconf-has-agent-p` / `agent-repl--agent-visible-in-current-ws-p` ask for the view alone; the extent tests in `test-status.el` then fail and name the ruling |

## The sweep's focus, and a capture for the quit that vanishes, 2026-09-13

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | Who hands focus back, now that a press may not | The SWEEP: `bin/realtest.sh` takes focus before its first realtest and gives it back from its EXIT trap, and every press keeps it (`keydriver --keep-focus`) | Owner ruling: per-press handback flickered the desktop once per keystroke and gave no way to see that a run was still going. The trap is what makes a failed, panicked or interrupted sweep still return the desktop | Drop `--keep-focus` from `keyDriverArgs` and the two `TestSweepFocus*` calls from the script |
| 2026-09-13 | Where the take and the handback live, given they run in different processes | Two `go test -run` harness checks, like the leftover clean and the gap scan, with the token in `sweep-focus.txt` in the run directory | `swiftc` is invoked in exactly one place (`KeyDriver.Build`), and a second spelling of that compile in shell is the drift this harness spends its code avoiding. The taking process has exited by handback time, so what travels is a name | Compile the helper in the script and call it directly |
| 2026-09-13 | What names the application focus goes back to | Its bundle identifier where it has one, its pid otherwise, `none` when nothing was frontmost | A bundle identifier survives an application that relaunched during the run; a raw pid would hand focus to whatever inherited the number | Print only the pid from `focusToken` |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether a declined activation or a locked screen stops the sweep | No. It is REPORTED by the take and by every press's receipt, and the sweep runs | Refusing to collect a run's findings over the window server's mood throws away the run. A harness failure of its own — no helper, no token — DOES stop the take, and then no press keeps focus and no handback is owed | Make `TestSweepFocusTake` fail on `activated=declined` |
| 2026-09-13 | What a press that finds Emacs not frontmost should do | Re-activate it, report `refocused=yes` in the receipt, and still not hand focus back | It is the ordinary shape twice over: the owner clicked away, or the realtest just cold-started a new Emacs, which every cold-start realtest does | Refuse the press instead of re-activating |
| 2026-09-13 | Where focus is supposed to be at the end of a phase of presses | Under the sweep's policy, on Emacs; under no sweep, back where it started (`focusAfterPressesNote`) | The old assertion — focus is exactly where it started — would fail every phase of a sweep that is holding focus on purpose | Keep only the restore branch |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether focus somewhere else at the end of a phase is a finding | No, a note | The owner may click away at any moment, and the policy's answer to that is the next press re-taking focus and saying so | `t.Errorf` on the third branch |
| 2026-09-13 | When the unsolved quit's diagnostic capture is taken | Twice: once before the press with the prompt standing, and once by a single `emacsclient` eval immediately after the quiet window, before any other probe | Earlier turns the key in flight into an interrupt (`Chord.Interrupting`); later describes an editor this harness has already been running lisp in, which is the state under suspicion. It is one eval so the capture describes one instant | Remove `DeliveryEffect.AfterQuietWindow` |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether the capture may affect the verdict | No. Nothing in `confirm` reads it, and a test asserts the verdict is the same with and without it | The press is judged once (minibuffer.go). Evidence that moved the judgement would stop being evidence | Read the capture in `judgeDelivery` |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether one signalling field may cost the capture | No: every one of the eighteen is wrapped in its own `condition-case` | Nobody yet knows which reading is the interesting one, so losing seventeen over one is losing the evidence | Wrap the whole `concat` once |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether the failure note carries the capture or a path to it | The whole capture, verbatim, and the path beside it | The note is what a reader of a failing run sees; the plan's bar for this loop is evidence verbatim, and a path is not evidence | Print only the path |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether a press whose prompt DID close gets the note | No note, but the captures are still filed | Eighteen fields per healthy press would bury the presses that vanished; the comparison between a press that worked and one that did not is exactly what the next sweep needs on disk | Always append the note |

## The vanishing C-g, resolved (2026-09-13 13:31, lead)

Sweep 132xxx under the new focus policy (one steal per sweep, one
hand-back) passed all eight realtests, and every quit press dismissed its
prompt and was recorded. The quit diagnostic capture shows a clean picture
before and after. The cause was the per-press focus hand-back: the helper
posted the quit character and deactivated Emacs at once, and the quit
character, which the NS port handles specially rather than queueing as an
ordinary event, was lost in the deactivation while ordinary keys survived
it. The deferred-quit fixes (deferral never dropped, requeue at a standing
prompt) are kept: they were real defects the investigation exposed, and
they are covered by tests.

## Owner rulings, second batch (2026-09-13, owner)

1. Boot brings up every open workspace's session. A hibernated workspace's
   shim is brought back when the workspace is opened or switched to. While
   hibernated, the topbar still draws and says the workspace is hibernated
   (one topbar-level state, not per-cell arms). A session-less open
   workspace is an invariant violation.
2. The cold gate keeps asking after a revival, including after a
   pre-compacted hibernation, until ruled otherwise.
3. Sidebar row chevrons are hover-only (and shown on an expanded row). The
   details popup may extend out of the sidebar into the feed for room, but
   must never be clipped by the webapp window.
4. The sidecar's aged-unowned-spool `hold-expired` record is INFO.
5. `DaemonHoldTray.heading` (the "held (N)" counter text) is retired from
   the proto.
6. Store volume: not to be solved by narrowing discovery; analyze what is
   written and stop storing what is never served (proposal owed).

## Owner ruling: the cold gate has a floor (2026-09-13, owner)

No cold gate when the context the cold read would re-read is under
70,000 tokens: the session continues automatically. At or above 70,000
the gate asks as today. Applies to every lapse (hibernation revival,
daemon restart, resume after the TTL); a model change is unaffected by
the floor only if its own rule says so (the cache is per model, so a
switch re-reads everything regardless; the same floor applies there too
unless the owner rules otherwise).
Also ruled: hook attachments and total-token reminders are not stored
(docs/STORE-VOLUME-PROPOSAL.md item 1, first two kinds).
## Sidebar chevron and detail panel, owner ruling 3 (2026-09-13)

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | The ruling's "hover-only" also has to cover an EXPANDED row and keyboard focus, neither of which `.chev:hover` can see. One CSS selector list, or one JS-maintained fact? | ONE JS-MAINTAINED FACT: `drawExpandChevron` sets `data-shown` from a single predicate — pointer over the row, focus inside it, or `.open` — and the stylesheet reveals `.chev[data-shown]` and nothing else | Two sources for one appearance drift, and `:hover` cannot express "or this row is expanded"; `data-shown` is also the only form of the rule a jsdom test can assert, since the unit suite has no cascade | Replace the `[data-shown]` rule with `.row:hover .chev, .ws.open > .row .chev` and delete the pointer/focus listeners in `webapp/src/sidebar/row.ts` |
| 2026-09-13 | Hiding it with `display: none` would collapse the glyph's slot and shift the name on hover | `visibility: hidden`, as the ruling requires: the slot is kept and nothing moves | The name jumping sideways under the pointer is exactly the shift the ruling names | Set `display: none` on `.chev` |
| 2026-09-13 | The panel was in-flow inside `#ws-sidebar`, which sets `overflow: hidden`, inside `.sb-scroll`, which clips again — so it was rail-wide and croppable. Absolute, or fixed? | FIXED. Absolute positioning is still clipped by an ancestor's overflow; `position: fixed` escapes both clips, and no ancestor here is transformed, which is the one thing that would defeat it | The ruling says the panel may reach into the feed and may never be cut off by the window; nothing weaker than leaving the rail's clip achieves the first half | Restore the in-flow `margin` block on `#ws-sidebar .ws.open > .detail` and delete `placeRowDetail` (webapp/src/sidebar/row.ts) |
| 2026-09-13 | A second "keep it inside the window" arithmetic, or the topbar reveals' `clampReveal`? | `clampReveal`, imported from `src/topbar/clamp.js`: under the anchor, sliding left only as far as the right edge demands, height capped at what is left below | Its rules are exactly the ones the ruling states, and a second implementation of a clamp is the drift that helper was written to prevent | Give the sidebar its own placement arithmetic |
| 2026-09-13 | A fixed panel does not travel with the row that anchors it | The rail re-places every open row after each draw and on `resize` plus a CAPTURED `scroll`, and drops both listeners on dispose | A scroll inside the rail's own scroller does not bubble to the window, and the panel is anchored to a rectangle that scroll just moved | Remove the two `window.addEventListener` calls in `mountSidebar` |
| 2026-09-13 | Where a row drawn ALREADY expanded is placed, given a detached tree measures as zeros | In `mountSidebar`, immediately after `body.replaceChildren(drawn)` — the first moment the row is on the page | Placing during the draw would write the window's origin into every remembered expansion | Call the placement from `drawRosterRow` instead |

## Implementing the cold-gate floor (2026-09-13, lead)

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | Where the floor is enforced | In the shim, at both of its decision sites: `judgeCold` (the resume judgement) and `SetSessionModel`'s own per-model gate | The ruling covers a model change too, and `SetSessionModel` does not route through `judgeCold` — a floor in one place only would leave a switch at a threshold of 0 gating a 500-token context | Move the check into `judgeCold` alone |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether the floor is a configuration knob | No: a `const COLD_GATE_FLOOR_TOKENS = 70_000` in `engine/cold.ts`, citing the ruling | The shim has no configuration surface for thresholds — every environment variable it reads is a refusal rather than a default — and the brief forbids inventing a flag | Add an env var and a default |
| 2026-09-13 | What the wire carries | Nothing new: `SessionCold` already states `context_tokens`, and the floor only decides whether the message is sent | The daemon and webapp wording is unchanged, and `Fleet.RaiseColdGate` is a relay that decides nothing | Add a floor field to the proto |
| 2026-09-13 | How the mocked vendor seeds a gate-tripping context | `!cold-seed` now states 90,000 tokens through a new `AssistantOptions.contextTokens`, instead of the mock's ordinary ~25,000 | Every cold-gate fixture sat under the new floor, so the whole area would have gone green while testing nothing | Raise the mock's default usage for every scenario |

## Never-persisted residue kinds, 2026-09-13

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | The ruling says an explicit never-persisted list "in internal/convert, where `keys.go` routes hook attachments to residue"; the two kinds are minted on two different branches (`hookAttachment` and the attachment default) | The list lives in `internal/convert/neverpersist.go` and filters the entries `Converter.Line` returns, keyed on the `vendor_specific` KIND the converter itself minted | One choke point on the ruled string rather than two branch edits: a future producer of either kind cannot route around it, and the line still runs its own classification branch first, which is what keeps the discovery mandate intact | Move the checks into `attachmentLine`'s two branches and delete `dropNeverPersisted` (internal/convert/neverpersist.go) |
| 2026-09-13 | The ruling asks to check ledger absorption for a line that produced no entry, and to add a "seen, not stored" mark or state the gap | No mark added; the gap is stated instead. Offset absorption is the CURSOR, not the write ledger: `PollResult.Changed` and `CursorState.Offset` turn on the bytes read, the advance rides the same batch, and the ledger's one-row-per-applied-write index has no role in re-read prevention | A "seen, not stored" ledger row would re-grow, per dropped line, exactly the 204 MB the ledger already costs — to duplicate a guarantee the cursor already gives structurally. Two tail tests pin it (`TestABatchOfOnlyDroppedLinesStillAdvancesTheCursor`, `TestTheSecondPollAfterAnAllDroppedBatchRereadsNothing`) | Add a ledger arm for seen-not-stored write ids in shim-store and mint one per dropped line |
| 2026-09-13 | The ruling asks for the drop at DEBUG per line "following the catch-up window idiom", but that idiom demotes INFO records only while the window is open and restores INFO afterwards | The per-line drop is DEBUG ALWAYS, and only the SUMMARY follows the window: one INFO `residue-drop-summary` per file at the catch-up edge, with counts by kind | A dropped kind is the steady state — 37% of a transcript's records — so restoring INFO per line after catch-up would make the flood permanent instead of leveling it | Register `residue-drop` in `catchupOperations` and log the drop with `Log` instead of `LogVerbose` |
| 2026-09-13 | Existing convert tests used `hook_success` as the sample kind for hook KEYING and raw-fidelity assertions, which now produce no entry | Those tests move to `hook_cancelled`, an outcome kind that is still stored; the kept-whole table loses `hook_success` and `neverpersist_test.go` asserts its drop | The tests' subjects are the residue key and the verbatim carry, not the success kind; the ruled behavior change gets its own coverage rather than being folded into theirs | Restore `hook_success` in `internal/convert/attachment_test.go` and in the golden census |
| 2026-09-13 | "A served hook row on the stream plane is unaffected" cannot be observed from the sidecar, which never writes that row | Asserted as the sidecar's half: a dropped hook attachment mints nothing at all, and in particular nothing under `activity:` (`TestADroppedHookAttachmentMintsNothingInTheServedHookKeySpace`) | The file plane's only possible effect on the stream's row is writing into its key space, and the drop writes nothing anywhere | Add a cross-plane e2e assertion instead |
## Judgement calls implementing owner ruling 1, second batch (2026-09-13)

| date | question | call | why | rejected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-13 | `daemon.drain.run` reported `could not read the standing drain schedule {cause=context canceled}` as an ERROR when the daemon's own exit cancelled the loop's schedule read. Fix or re-level? | RE-LEVELED to debug for a cancelled read only | The rule landed at three other sites today (464ff5325): the serving context ending under work in flight is this daemon leaving, not a failure, and there is no drain left to fire; the read is a loop-internal step nothing acts on, so debug rather than info | Restore the unconditional `c.log.Error` in `Run` (daemon/internal/drain/sweep.go); `TestRunLevelsTheScheduleReadByWhetherTheLoopWasCancelled` then fails |
| 2026-09-13 | Which shape carries the topbar's hibernated state, the oneof or the message field the ruling offered | `TopbarHibernated hibernated = 11`, a message field with presence | The file's own header rule is that every field of the view is an element message, so an element the state suppresses is expressed by not setting it; a oneof over the whole view would restate that partition a second time for one state | A `oneof state` wrapping the other nine fields |
| 2026-09-13 | Which readiness gates a PARKED workspace's topbar keeps | The naming and the account | Those two are workspace facts (WSM's and the config root's); the other three are session facts a stood-down session will never state, so gating on them is the blank strip the hibernated view exists to replace | Publish the hibernated view with no gate at all |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether the warning strip is drawn while hibernated | No — it is session-scoped | Every arm of it (accounting, unmodeled tools, session faults, degraded windows) is resolved from session facts, so a parked workspace has none to state | Keep publishing an empty strip |
| 2026-09-13 | Which set the boot's bring-up walks: "not adopted", or "client-less" | Client-less, the set `adopt` already answers | `adopt` answers three sets, and UNDETERMINED — the lock or socket probe could not tell — is never read as free anywhere in that sequence; spawning a second shim onto a conversation a survivor may still own is the exact loss that discipline prevents | Start every open row with no adopted client |
| 2026-09-13 | Where in the boot sequence the bring-up runs | Last, after the merges are recovered | Every earlier step reconciles state a freshly spawned session would race: the orphaned turns are closed, the holds are restored, the merges are recovered | Immediately after `adopt`, as the ruling's wording reads |
| 2026-09-13 | What a failed session-record read does to the bring-up of that workspace | Counts as a bring-up failure, at ERROR, and the next workspace is still started | "Could not tell" is never the benign answer here either: reading it as "not hibernated" would spawn over a deliberate sleep, and reading it as hibernated would leave a workspace session-less with nobody able to say why | Treat an unreadable record as not parked |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether Select's revival failure fails the select | Yes | A selected workspace with no session is the state this ruling abolishes, and answering success would hide it | Log and carry on with the selection |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether the relaunch integration test's assertion or its arrangement moved | Its arrangement: the shim profile is written before the successor starts | The successor now brings sessions up during its own boot, so a fixture written after `StartDaemon` returned is read by nothing. No assertion changed | Relax the page assertion, or delay the boot bring-up |
## Agent judgement calls (2026-09-13, sandboxed Emacs layer creates)

1. The Emacs e2e layer's daemon gets the SAME fake vendor binary the daemon's
   own integration harness writes (`harness.NewFakeClaude`), named explicitly
   on `AGENT_REPL_CLAUDE_BIN`. The layer already exported
   `AGENT_REPL_FORBID_VENDOR_CALLS=1` and named no binary, so every nameless
   create's headless naming call was refused by the guard. The naming call is
   NOT weakened and the guard is NOT relaxed: an explicitly named binary is
   already the documented legal spawn (`daemon/AGENTS.md`), and it is what the
   Go worlds have always done (`e2e/SPEC.md` seam 2). Reusing the harness's
   fake rather than writing a second one keeps the naming contract in one
   place, so the slug a nameless create produces is the same
   `harness.FakeClaudeMintedName` in both layers.
2. The teardown's daemon-stop acceptance check is matched against the arm
   plist `(:arm :accepted)` rather than a bare `t`.
   `agent-repl-frontend-daemon-stop` documents an arm for its on-done
   callback, so the harness was reading a shape production no longer answers
   with and logged every healthy teardown as unaccepted. The check is
   TIGHTENED, not dropped: the accepted arm is matched by name, so a
   `(:arm :not-restarted ...)` refusal still reports.

| date | question | call | why | rejected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-13 | Which state the strip names when a cold gate stands on a workspace the idle sweep has also parked | The cold gate | A standing gate is waiting on the READER — the feed is showing a card that must be answered before the workspace can do anything — while hibernation waits on nothing and lifts itself on the next prompt; the park is remembered underneath and draws again the moment the gate is answered | Hibernation wins because the park is the older fact; or drawing both cells |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether the topbar's cold-gate cell restates the gate's menu (pay, clear, compact) | No — the label, the context figure and the age only | The feed's gate card is where the gate is answered, and a second place to answer one question is exactly what the strip must not become | Mirror the card's buttons into the strip |
| 2026-09-13 | How the cold-gate state reaches the topbar resolver | A `SetColdGate(ws, ColdGate)` seam called from the SAME sites that raise the feed row and the footer status (`Fleet.raiseColdGate`, `verbs.AnswerColdGate`) | One gate must not become three answers; the footer's own `SetColdGate` established the shape | Have the topbar read the fleet's `ColdGate` for itself |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether the two sessionless exemptions from the readiness gate are written twice | No — both run through one `wsState.sessionless()` predicate | A third session-scoped gate added to one state and forgotten in the other is the drift the single predicate makes impossible | Repeat `s.parked || s.coldGate` at each of the four sites |
| 2026-09-13 | Which log level the cold-gate publication and the return to the full view take | Info, both | Same reason the incomplete-topbar record was raised to info the same day: a strip missing its whole session-scoped half must be diagnosable without a reproduction | Debug, as the ordinary republication is |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether the webapp validates the cold gate's token count itself | No — it calls the feed gate card's own `tokenCountOf` | Two validators for one count is the drift a shared reader prevents; a negative or unsafe count is a `MalformedView` wherever it lands | A second bounds check in the topbar module |
