## SIGTERM with a batch in flight, 2026-09-13

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | `TestSigtermCommitsNoCursorPastRecordsItDidNotStore` failed intermittently under parallel load with the store's cursor past two units its book did not hold; the brief called it a data-loss race in the shutdown path | It is not data loss. Instrumented repro: the sidecar exited at 14:37:04.9244, the book read at .9696 was missing a unit, the cursor read at .9740 was already past it, and a re-read of the same book at .9748 held it — the store committed the withdrawn write ~50ms after the process was gone, and the subject's two rpcs straddled that commit | The store commits a batch's records and its cursor advance in ONE transaction (shim-store internal/db/write.go), and `ReadPage` reads those rows directly with no projection lag, so no reader ever sees the torn state the subject reported — only a reader that joins two separate snapshots can manufacture it | Read the sidecar log dumps under `scratchpad/hunt5_2.log` and re-run `go test -race -count=3 -parallel 16 ./integration/` on the parent commit |
| 2026-09-13 | Given that, is there anything to fix in production, or is this only the subject's read order? | Both. The subject's read order is fixed, AND the shutdown now gives a sent write 500ms to answer before cancelling it (`shutdownSettle`) | Cancelling a write already on the wire does not recall it — the store commits it anyway and net/http tells the handler the client is gone only after this process has exited. The instant cancel therefore destroyed only the sidecar's own knowledge of the outcome, and left the store committing a batch nobody was waiting for | Make `rpcContext` a plain `context.WithTimeout(s.shutdown, rpcTimeout)` again (cycle.go) |
| 2026-09-13 | The shutdown record said the interrupted write "will replay", and two subjects asserted that wording | The record now says the outcome is unknown and states why nothing is lost either way; `cycle_test.go` and `sigterm_wedged_write_test.go` match on the new prefix | The old sentence asserted as fact something the process cannot know, and it is the sentence that made an ordinary torn read look like data loss to everyone who read the failure | Restore the "it will replay" wording in `storeWrite` and both test constants |
| 2026-09-13 | Which read order does each direction of the subject need? | "No record ahead of the cursor" reads the BOOK first; "no cursor past unstored records" reads the CURSOR first. `terminateMidIngest` takes the order as an argument rather than picking one for both | Each direction must read the half that can only GROW last, or a write committing between the two rpcs spells a violation the store never held. Neither ordering weakens the subject: a real advance past unstored records is permanent and both reads see it | Drop the `readOrder` argument and read the book first for both |
| 2026-09-13 | `shutdownSettle` is 500ms | Kept at 500ms | A healthy WriteBatch answers in single-digit milliseconds, so this is two orders of magnitude of headroom, and it is an order of magnitude below the 3s a signalled sidecar's shutdown is held to by `wedgedShutdownBudget` | Change the constant in cycle.go |

## Owner rulings, third batch (2026-09-13, owner)

- Stop storing `queue-operation` and `file-history-snapshot` residue too.
- Raw-bytes residue storage is fine if it is not lossy.
- Do NOT narrow sidecar discovery; it would only hide inefficiency.
- The store gets a separate read connection pool (reads never queue on writes).
- A workspace whose repository is unregistered is an INVARIANT VIOLATION:
  it must be impossible, not drawn in a special section.
- Fix the handover race only if it can be reproduced and verified, not
  speculated.
- Docker Desktop: reset/reinstall freely; nothing there matters.
## The door a harness-ordered daemon stop goes through, 2026-09-13

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | The sweep's three daemon stops sent a bare SIGTERM and the successor logged `daemon.rollout.reconcile` "sessions survived a bounce that wrote no intent manifest" four times; which door replaces it? | `UpdateShutdownSchedule{now}` with an operator reason, the same rpc the editor's `agent-repl-frontend-daemon-stop` sends and therefore the one `bin/deploy-all.sh` reaches through `agent-repl-runtime-restart-await` | SIGTERM only cancels the serving context: no session is stood down, so every shim survives and the next daemon adopts processes nothing stated an intent for. The `now` handler runs `standEverySessionDown` and `sweepInFlightSpawns` before it exits, so the successor adopts nothing and logs an ordinary boot | Restore the `kill -TERM` body of `stop_daemons_orderly` (bin/realtest.sh) |
| 2026-09-13 | Emacs, or the socket? | The socket, directly, with no Emacs involved | Two of the three call sites have just QUIT the owner's editor — the preflight quits it before stopping the daemon so a live editor cannot bring an unguarded one straight back up — so an editor-mediated stop is unavailable at exactly the moments the stop is needed | Make `stop_daemons_orderly` call `emacsclient --eval '(agent-repl-frontend-daemon-stop)'` when an Emacs answers |
| 2026-09-13 | Bash or Go for the rpc? | Go: `TestOrderlyDaemonStop` in `e2e/realtest`, run through the same `run_harness_check` the leftover check and the gap scan use | The door is a Connect rpc over h2c and the generated client is already on that module's path; a hand-spelled frame in bash would be a second implementation of the wire contract, which is the drift `leftovers.go` already exists to prevent | Spell the Connect frame with `curl` inside bin/realtest.sh |
| 2026-09-13 | What happens when the daemon does not answer its own door? | SIGTERM, still, and the run states both that it fell back and what the fallback costs | A daemon left standing defeats the run's whole purpose (a stack whose daemon carries the guard), so the signal cannot simply be withheld; what must never happen again is taking it silently | Make an unanswered door a decline instead of a fallback (bin/realtest.sh) |
| 2026-09-13 | Does the harness wait for the daemon to go, or does the Go check? | The shell, off `DAEMON_STOP_SECONDS`, exactly as before; the Go check bounds only the conversation (`daemonStopBound`, 30s) | Only the caller knows what a daemon still standing means for it — a preflight decline, a realtest 3 skip, or a handback warning — and moving the wait into Go would split one bound across two languages | Move the exit wait into `StopDaemonOrderly` (e2e/realtest/daemonstop.go) |
## The store's writer-versus-writer contention, 2026-09-13

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | The store's own two writers were colliding on `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`; raise `busy_timeout`, or serialize in process? | Serialize: a one-slot `writeGate` in `internal/db`, with `beginWrite` the only way a write transaction is opened | A bigger timeout only moves where the refusal lands — the loser still burns the whole window and still loses the batch to a retry — while the store is by design the single writer PROCESS, so the arbitration belongs in it and a queued batch can simply wait its turn | Delete `internal/db/writer.go` and call `d.sql.BeginTx` directly in `WriteBatch` |
| 2026-09-13 | A one-slot channel or a `sync.Mutex` for the gate? | A channel | Only a channel can be selected against `ctx.Done()`, which is what lets a caller that hangs up while queued receive its own cancellation instead of a storage failure its producer would retry for a caller that is gone | Swap `writeGate` for a `sync.Mutex` and drop the cancellation arm in `WriteBatch` |
| 2026-09-13 | Keep the `SQLITE_BUSY` storage-failure refusal now that no sibling can cause one? | Keep it | Nothing else opens the file BY DESIGN, but `sqlite3` at a shell, a second store racing the socket singleton check, or a backup tool all still can; the gate's claim is only that a BUSY never came from this process | Drop the `storagef(err, "begin write transaction")` arm |
| 2026-09-13 | `lock_wait_ms` was sub-millisecond in a test, so the exact wait could not be asserted without sleeping | Add `DB.mono`, an injectable monotonic clock, and advance it from the queue hook | A wait asserted as `>= 0` proves nothing, and a real wait long enough to round to milliseconds is a sleep by another name; the injectable clock makes the number exact and the test instant | Revert `Options.Clock` and read `time.Now` directly at each timing site |

## The store's read pool, 2026-09-13 (owner ruling)

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | `mode=ro` or a plain DSN with `query_only(true)` for the read pool? | A read-write file open with `query_only(true)`, and no `_txlock` | `mode=ro` cannot create or recover the WAL `-shm` file, so a read pool that opened first — or after a crash — would fail on a database the write handle can open perfectly well; `query_only` restricts the STATEMENTS without restricting the file | Change `readDSN` to carry `mode=ro` (internal/db/db.go) |
| 2026-09-13 | The read DSN's pragma list: keep `journal_mode(WAL)`? | Dropped from the read DSN; `busy_timeout`, `foreign_keys` and `query_only` remain, with `query_only` LAST | The journal mode is a durable property of the FILE that the write handle already established, and setting it needs write access `query_only` denies; ordering `query_only` last keeps it from refusing the pragmas ahead of it | Add `journal_mode(WAL)` back to `readDSN` |
| 2026-09-13 | Keep `beginRead`'s `sql.TxOptions{ReadOnly: true}` now that the pool cannot reach the write lock? | Keep it | It states the intent at the call site and costs nothing; removing working coverage because a second mechanism now also covers it is how a guarantee quietly becomes single-point | Pass `nil` options in `beginRead` (internal/db/read.go) |
| 2026-09-13 | How does a test prove a read does not wait on a write, with no sleep and no timer? | The read runs SYNCHRONOUSLY while the write transaction is held and the single write connection is taken | If a read path ever reaches the write pool again the test does not fail slowly, it never returns, and the package timeout reports it — verified by temporarily pointing `beginRead` back at the write handle | Move the read onto its own goroutine with a select (internal/db/read_test.go) |

## The write ledger's retention rule, 2026-09-13 (owner ruling)

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | The store cannot open a `write_id` and `PlaneFile` carries no coordinates, so how does a ledger row learn its source position without a proto change? | It is stamped with the BATCH's `cursor_advance` — the file id, and the offset the producer's NEXT read starts at | That offset is strictly above every offset the batch's own rows came from, so it is an UPPER bound on the row's true position and `cursor - recorded` is a LOWER bound on its true distance behind; every error it can make is in the direction of keeping a row too long | Drop `source_file_id`/`source_offset` from `write_ledger` and the sweep with them (internal/db/db.go, prune.go) |
| 2026-09-13 | Retention window equal to the sidecar's 4 MB rewind bound, or wider? | 16 MB, four times over | The two constants live in different modules and are bumped by different people; a window that merely matched today's bound would start losing absorptions the moment somebody widened the scan, and a ledger row is ~100 bytes against 4 MB of transcript | Set `DefaultLedgerRetentionBytes` to `4 << 20` (internal/db/prune.go) |
| 2026-09-13 | Prune stream-plane ledger rows too? | No — a row with no source file is kept | The shim re-emits from an in-memory retry buffer whose bound the store cannot see, so there is no structural argument for a cutoff, and an age-based guess is exactly the probabilistic rule this codebase refuses | Add an `applied_at_ms` arm to the sweep's WHERE clause |
| 2026-09-13 | The coordinator's rule included "the file is gone"; implement it? | Not taken; a row whose file has no cursor row is KEPT | Deciding it means the store stat()ing vendor files it otherwise never touches, and no cursor row means that file is re-read FROM ZERO — the case where the ledger is doing the most work — to save rows a store reset throws away wholesale anyway | Add a path stat to `pruneLedgerBatch` and prune rows whose file is absent |
| 2026-09-13 | New ledger columns: a migration, or the nuke? | The nuke: `SchemaVersion` 5 to 6 | The store's convention IS the nuke — there is no `ALTER` anywhere in the package and writing one is forbidden — and the bump also disposes of the 204 MB ledger that prompted this | Revert `SchemaVersion` and add the columns with an `ALTER` (forbidden) |
| 2026-09-13 | How does a test prove the sweep never blocks a producer, with no sleeps? | The probe write runs from INSIDE the sweep, through the `afterPruneBatch` seam, after a batch has committed and released | It proves the slot is free MID-sweep rather than only at the end; if the sweep ever held it across the whole run the probe never returns and the package timeout says so | Move the probe outside the sweep and assert only that both finished (internal/db/prune_test.go) |


## The unregistered-repository invariant (2026-09-13)

Implementing the owner's third-batch ruling that "a workspace whose repository
is unregistered is an INVARIANT VIOLATION: it must be impossible, not drawn in
a special section".

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | The brief asks for a foreign key from `workspaces.repo` to `repositories` "with the layout's migration convention". There already is one. Add a migration anyway? | NO migration. The constraint is pinned by tests instead, and the column now says at the schema what it is holding | `workspaces.repo_id` has been `NOT NULL REFERENCES repositories(id)` since the layout landed (schema.go, and the frozen `testdata/layout3.sql` declares it too), and every handle `Open` hands out has always carried `_pragma=foreign_keys(1)`. A layout-7 step rebuilding the table to re-add a constraint that is already there is pure risk against the user's data, for no change in what SQLite enforces | Append a step to `migrations` in migrate.go that rebuilds `workspaces` |
| 2026-09-13 | Then where could a violating row come from at all? | A handle opened WITHOUT the pragma — foreign keys are per-connection, and the `sqlite3` CLI defaults them off — so a hand repair or a restored backup can leave one behind. The open now reports it | A row like that reads back perfectly through every getter and then vanishes out of the roster's repository grouping, which is exactly the shape of the finding. Nothing in this package can write one | Delete the `checkRepositoryInvariant` call in `Open` (open.go) |
| 2026-09-13 | Does the boot check REPAIR or REPORT? | REPORT: one ERROR naming every violating row and the remedy, and the open proceeds | The brief allows either and forbids a silent delete. The workspace state is the user's data and is carried forward, never thrown away (migrate.go); a row nobody can see is still a row someone can re-register. Refusing the open would serve nothing at all, which is strictly worse than booting and saying what is wrong | Return the refusal from `checkRepositoryInvariant` instead of nil (repoinvariant.go) |
| 2026-09-13 | "Refuse a create naming an unregistered repository" — the CreateWorkspace rpc already does, on `unknown_repository`. Is there anything left to do? | Yes: the check MOVES to the verb. The rpc's `RepositoryRef` lookup was one of three ways into `Create`, and the command-file channel's bare `git_root` never passed through it | An agent-written `git_root` naming any directory on disk minted a brand-new repository row for it, with no user ever having chosen that repository — and the roster's repository sections ARE the create targets. Putting it on the verb makes the endpoint's lookup a redundancy rather than the only guard | Delete the `repositoryRegisteredAt` block in `Create` (create.go) |
| 2026-09-13 | That refusal broke three command-file integration subjects that created against a repository nothing had registered. Amend the subjects, or back the change out? | Amend: each now calls `harness.Register(t, d, repo.Dir)` first | The state they arranged is one the CreateWorkspace rpc has NEVER allowed, so they were the only place in the suite where a create ran against an unregistered tree; the idiom they now use is the one the rest of the same file already uses, and it is what the editor does when it opens a repository. No assertion was weakened — every one of the three still asserts the create materializes exactly as `CreateWorkspace` does — and a new subject pins the refusal and the quarantine | Drop the three `harness.Register` calls (integration/commandfile_test.go) |
| 2026-09-13 | `RegisterWorkspace` was to "register the repository row first". Did it? | It already did, and now a test says so | `ensureRepo` runs before the workspace INSERT inside the same transaction (workspaces.go), so the ordering was never in doubt; what was missing was a subject that would fail if it were reversed | Delete `TestRegisterWorkspaceWritesTheRepositoryRowBeforeTheWorkspace` |
| 2026-09-13 | `repositoryView` "asserts the invariant, an internal error surfaced, not a silent drop". Should the roster build return an error and publish nothing? | No. The orphan branch becomes a NAMED assertion (`assertRepositoryInvariant`), keeping the ERROR record and the drop | `render` has no error channel and `mutate` publishes whatever it returns, so an error would have to become "publish no roster at all" — every workspace invisible instead of one, over a state three layers now make unreachable. The assertion is the same shape as `assertArm` beside it, and it quotes the state store's own remedy so the two logs say the same thing | Give `render`/`repositoryView` an error return and refuse the publication (resolver.go, sections.go) |
| 2026-09-13 | The ruling asks for a line "slightly darker" than the expanded section's row separator. Which color? | A new `--border-strong: color-mix(in srgb, var(--border) 85%, var(--fg))`, derived from the row separator's own `--border` and used only by the footer divider so far | The row delimiters come from ONE shared rule (`.list-rows > * + *`, `1px solid var(--border)`), so the partition must be the same grey one step on rather than a second unrelated color; deriving it means the two lines cannot drift apart when a theme moves `--border`. 15% is the smallest mix that still reads as a heavier line at 1px (light: #e5e7eb -> ~#c7c8cb) | Hard-code a hex for the divider, or reuse `--border` and let the partition read the same weight as the rows inside it |
| 2026-09-13 | The mix is toward `--fg`, not toward black. Does "darker" survive the dark theme? | Yes, and deliberately: one derivation for both themes | `--fg` is near-black in the light theme (the mix darkens the grey) and pale in the dark theme (the mix lifts it off the dark ground), so the SAME declaration is one step stronger in each; a literal black mix would render the dark theme's partition all but invisible, which is the opposite of what the ruling asks the line to do | Redefine `--border-strong` per theme, or mix toward `#000000` in both |
| 2026-09-13 | Where does the line live: a border on the strip, a border on the section, or an element? | Its own `.footer-divider` element, a child of `.pfooter` between the two, drawn only when a panel is open | The ruling asks for a line that spans the footer's full width with no side inset, and the expanded section carries 10px of its own side padding — a border on it would be inset; a bare element under the dock has neither padding nor margin, so it partitions edge to edge. Drawn only when a panel is open because a closed footer is one section | Put `border-top` back on `.pfooter-sheet` (its old rule, removed here so the partition is not doubled) |

## Owner ruling: footer expanded section (2026-09-13, owner)

The footer's expanded section draws UNDER the main strip, not over it.
The two sections are separated by a line that is slightly darker than the
line separating rows inside the expanded section, and that line spans the
footer's full width, left edge to right edge, so it fully partitions the
two sections. (The token-usage figures in the footer are known to be
wrong as currently described; deferred.)

## Owner ruling: the unmodelled-line shape catalog (2026-09-13, owner)

Unmodelled transcript lines are not stored as residue, but their SHAPE is:
a separate table, one row per distinct recursive key structure (hash of
the key names, recursively), holding the first example seen verbatim, the
line kind, first/last seen and a count, so the vendor's API can be
discovered later. The module AGENTS.md documents the catalog.
## Sidecar gap scan, 2026-09-13 15:28

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | Can the sidecar tell a file deleted with its whole tree from one unlinked under the reader? | Yes — it stats the parent directory at the moment the read fails (`treeRemoved`, cycle.go) | The two cases are the same syscall error and different facts: a directory removed on purpose means the committed offset is the last thing the file had, while a standing directory means bytes may have been taken from under us | Delete `treeRemoved` and pass `false` to `MarkVanished` (cycle.go) |
| 2026-09-13 | Does `tree_removed` become a fourth LOST arm? | No. The wire arm stays `file_vanished`; the tree removal changes only the record LEVEL and rides the `file-vanished` record's `reason` key | `lost-terminal` joins back to the sweep on `reason` (seam.go), so a second vocabulary in that key on a `lost-policy` record would break the join that exists to explain a terminal | Add `ReasonTreeRemoved` to internal/stale and map it to the same `DetachedLost` arm |
| 2026-09-13 | Does a stat that fails for a reason OTHER than absence count as a removed tree? | No — only `fs.ErrNotExist` | A permission change or an unresponsive mount is not evidence the tree was removed, and reading it as one silently downgrades a genuine unlink to an ordinary end | Answer true for any stat error in `treeRemoved` |
| 2026-09-13 | The brief expected the 14:55-14:56 warns to be realtest run directories the harness deletes wholesale; they are agent task spools under the session scratchpad, whose `tasks/` directory STANDS | The discriminator lands anyway and those six records keep their warning | The rule is right and the wider corpus is overwhelmingly tree removals (of the `file-vanished` records in the live log, the large majority name a parent that is gone: deleted session `subagents/` subtrees and `workflows/wf_*` directories); the scan's own six are the genuine-unlink case the warning exists for | Widen the rule to any vanished file, which is the loss-blind behavior this replaces |
| 2026-09-13 | The brief read the ten `resolve-transcript-workspace` warns as a roster answer — a transcript whose directory is not a registered workspace | It is not the roster. Every one of the ten reads `open ... no such file or directory`: the attribution read opens the transcript ITSELF, and all ten files (one `.claude-chesscom` main session and its `subagents/` subtree, across four projects) were deleted at 14:37 | The record's own error text names the failing syscall, and the session directories are absent on disk today; the daemon roster was never consulted, because attribution comes from the transcript's first `cwd` line | Re-read the raw messages in `~/.cache/agent-repl/log/shim-claude-sidecar.log.1` around 14:37:31 |
| 2026-09-13 | Given that, info or warn? | Info, `reason=transcript_vanished`, once per file, summarized as backlog | Nothing was skipped: attribution is the first thing done to a discovered transcript, so a file gone before it leaves no unread bytes and nothing to wait for. A transcript that is PRESENT and unattributable is a different fact and keeps its warning | Delete the `fs.ErrNotExist` branch in `resolveTranscriptWorkspace` (held.go) |
| 2026-09-13 | Six `storeclient-cursors` ERRORs read "canceled: context canceled"; is that the store or this process? | This process. Every one sits inside a shutdown (pid 35018 at 14:19:00/01 seconds before its `exit`, pid 28201 at 14:36:41 among its own `shutdown interrupted a write` records) | `rpcContext` derives the call context from the shutdown context, so cancellation can only be self-inflicted; a store that failed to answer in time returns `DeadlineExceeded`, which keeps its error | Read the surrounding lines in `shim-claude-sidecar.log.1` at those timestamps |
| 2026-09-13 | Where is the withdrawal narrated — the client, or the caller? | The caller. `storeclient` drops to the per-call debug detail; `cursorFor` states one INFO `shutdown` record | Exactly-once: the client is a layer below anything that knows a shutdown is running, and two records for one fact is what the rule forbids. This is the same split commit fd8105ee0 settled on the write verb | Log the cancellation at error in `Client.Cursors` again |

## Residue is never persisted, 2026-09-13

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | Where does the residue rule live — at each producer, or at the write path? | At the write path: `withholdResidue`, immediately above `storeWrite` | Residue is minted by the converter, by three handlers and by the detached-stop seam, so a filter at any producer is a filter the next producer forgets; `storeWrite` is the sidecar's only door to the store, so a rule enforced there is a rule about the sidecar | Move the filter back into `Converter.Line` (internal/convert) |
| 2026-09-13 | Is `keepalive` residue? | No. It rides the same `unserved_item` field and is still persisted | It is a well-formed conversation fact with no book, not something the reader could not carry; `IsResidue` names the three arms explicitly rather than testing the field | Make `IsResidue` return true for any `unserved_item` |
| 2026-09-13 | With no stored `unknown` row, what carries forward-compatibility? | The classification, the per-record `residue-drop` label and the per-file summary | The sidecar's sources are the vendor's own DURABLE files, so nothing is unrecoverable: the day a residue arm earns a model, the file is re-read from the store's cursor. A stored row was never the only copy | Restore a named-kind list and persist everything not on it |
| 2026-09-13 | Does a batch whose every record was residue still write? | It still writes its CURSOR ADVANCE; a batch left with no entries and no advance is not sent at all | The bytes were read and re-reading them produces the same nothing, so the position must become durable — but an inferred batch that was entirely residue has nothing for the store to do, and asking anyway spends an rpc per residue line | Send every batch unconditionally in `storeWrite` |
| 2026-09-13 | The `residue-drop` record used to carry the withheld entry's `upsert_key` | It carries the residue label on `reason` and no key | A record naming an upsert_key announces a STORE ROW, and the field-set contract requires such a record to carry path, file_id and offset so the row is traceable — but there is no row, so naming a key nobody can look up is exactly the untraceable announcement the contract forbids | Put `UpsertKey` back on the record and add path/offset to the batch it is read from |
| 2026-09-13 | The gap finding said the restart path does not go through `agent-repl-frontend-daemon-stop`; it does | Kept the single request site and made the order name its addressee (`agent-repl-daemon--exit-requested` holds the process, or t for an adopted daemon) | The real cause is the finding's own second alternative: `--await-departure` keys on the boot claim, which the kernel frees when the process ends, so the ensure spawns and the spawn's blanket clear wipes the order before the predecessor's sentinel runs. Adding a second request site would have left that race in place | Set the flag again inside `agent-repl-frontend-daemon-restart` and in `agent-repl-runtime-restart-await` |

## The unmodelled-line shape catalog, 2026-09-13

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | Where does the shape observation get taken — in the converter, where the line was decoded, or at the write door? | At the write door, `cycle.go withholdResidue` | Residue is minted by the converter, by three handlers and by the detached-stop seam; the coordinating ruling put the withholding at the one door for exactly that reason, and a catalog that observed only what the converter minted would silently miss the spool handlers' `unparsed` bytes | Move the call into `Converter.Line` and add three more at the other producers |
| 2026-09-13 | `first_example` is ruled "the raw line bytes, verbatim", but the write door sits above the converter and no longer holds the frame's bytes | The example is the residue arm's OWN payload: the vendor's bytes for `unparsed`, and the decoded payload re-serialized as compact JSON for `vendor_specific` and `unknown` | It agrees with the vendor's line in every key and every value and differs only in key order and whitespace — nothing is added and nothing is dropped. Carrying the true bytes instead would mean threading the frame through the converter and all four producers, which is the plumbing the one-door ruling exists to avoid. FLAGGED FOR THE OWNER as the one place this departs from the ruling's wording | Add a raw-bytes field to `StoreEntry`'s residue arms, populate it at every producer, and read it here |
| 2026-09-13 | Is a batch that is nothing but withheld residue still sent? | Yes — carrying no entries and only its shape observations. A batch with no entries, no cursor advance AND no shapes is still not sent | The observation is the one durable thing the withheld line leaves behind, and one dropped for looking like an empty batch is a shape no re-read ever observes again: the cursor advance that consumed its bytes may commit in a later batch. This supersedes the "a batch of only residue is not even sent" row above, whose premise was that the store had nothing to do with it | Restore the `len(shapes) == 0` term to nothing in `storeWrite`'s early return |
| 2026-09-13 | How does the catalog avoid becoming a copy of the corpus when a map is keyed by tool-call id? | Object keys matching the generated-id rule collapse to one wildcard `*`, and several id keys in one map merge under it | Without it every id-keyed line is its own structure and the row count grows with traffic, which is the one thing the catalog must not do. The rule is narrow on purpose — hex runs only at 16 characters or more, prefixed ids only with something after the prefix — because a shape whose vocabulary keys were guessed away describes no API | Delete `generatedID` and key every object verbatim |
| 2026-09-13 | Do bytes that are not JSON get a shape? | They share one `<unparsable>` row, whose example is the first such line | They have no key structure to catalogue, and one row that says so with an example a human can read is more useful than none | Return `false` from `ResidueShape` for the unparsed arm |
| 2026-09-13 | Does the catalog get a retention rule like `write_ledger`? | No, by the owner's ruling: none during development | The row count is bounded by the number of distinct shapes a vendor emits rather than by traffic, so there is nothing for a sweep to remove | Add a `residue_shapes` sweep beside `prune.go` |

## The forced stop under a stand-down this daemon ordered, 2026-09-13

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | Where is the stand-down latch armed on the escalation path? | At the verb, before the ask: `Fleet.KillSession` and `verbs.kill` call the new `Shim.StandDown()` ahead of `shim.KillSession`, so it is armed before the unconditional process stop that a refusal leads to | The rpc arms the latch only if it reaches the shim client at all; a shim that never answers, or a client the ask cannot reach, left the escalation's own stop looking like a departure nobody ordered — which is the `daemon.shimclient.exit` ERROR and the four `daemon.shimclient.redial` WARNs the gap scan found | Drop the `StandDown()` calls and let `client.KillSession`/`client.Kill` be the only arming sites |
| 2026-09-13 | Is "the session kill did not answer; stopping the process anyway" still a WARN? | INFO when this daemon ordered the stand-down, WARN otherwise | The escalation IS the verb's contract — a shim that will not answer is stopped anyway — so the record is the mechanism working, not a defect. A kill outside a stand-down this daemon ordered is a different event and stays loud | Restore the unconditional `log.Warn` at both sites |
| 2026-09-13 | What makes a kill "outside a stand-down this daemon ordered", now that the verb arms the latch itself? | A `StandDown()` that answers false, which is exactly the DETACHED client | A detached client's process belongs to the successor daemon, so this daemon is ordering no teardown of it — the same rule `Kill` already applies when it refuses a detached client with `ErrDetached` | Make `client.StandDown` arm unconditionally and always answer true |
## The `SessionStart:resume` hook that hung every boot bring-up, 2026-09-13

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | Which layer bounds the wait for the vendor's `init` — the daemon, which already has a 60s bring-up bound, or the shim? | Both, with the shim's bound the SMALLER one (`INIT_TIMEOUT_MS` 45s) | Only the shim knows WHY the start did not finish; the daemon can only say the shim did not answer. A shim bound under the daemon's means the named reason wins the race and the daemon relays it | Drop `INIT_TIMEOUT_MS` and leave `initTimeoutMs` injected only by suites |
| 2026-09-13 | Should the start gate look only at `SessionStart` hooks, or at any hook that blocks before `init`? | Any hook | The only hooks that can fire before the vendor announces the session ARE its `SessionStart` ones, so a matcher on the event name adds a way to be wrong and no discrimination | Add `hook_event === "SessionStart"` to `settleStartOnBlockingHook` |
| 2026-09-13 | Does a `SessionStart` hook that merely FAILS — the owner's grounded case is the plugin's `powershell` arm with no interpreter on macOS — fail the start? | No. Only a hook with BLOCKING TEXT does | `hookBlockingText` is the settled reading of that difference and already governs how the hook is drawn; a failing hook gates nothing, and treating stderr as a refusal would fail every boot on a hook the vendor itself ignored | Make `hookBlockingText` return `stderr` when `output` is empty |
| 2026-09-13 | Does a blocked start get its own `StartSession` failure arm? | No — `vendor_start_failed`, with the hook and its text in `detail` | The proto documents that arm as "the SDK did not get us to a first message", `detail` as "the shim's account, for a human and for logs; never switched on", and the daemon relays it by name. Nothing switches on a blocked-by-hook start, so a new arm would be a wire change with no reader | Add a `hook_blocked` arm to `shim.v1 StartSessionFailure` and the daemon's `OpenWorkspaceError` |

## Owner rulings: topbar layout (2026-09-13, owner)

- The topbar spans the full width, left edge to right edge, with edge
  padding equal to the padding between cells.
- All free width pads the CENTER section, which is centered on its CONTENT
  against the full span (the middle character of "bob" sits at the exact
  middle of the strip), never pushed by the left or right groups.
- The connectivity glyph sits to the LEFT of the account label and close
  to it: it is the account's status indicator, one element.
- Ideally the center shows the workspace SUMMARY rather than the name, if
  the vendor/SDK provides one (diagnosed: the vendor writes an `ai-title`
  transcript line; not on our protos yet).
- The whole-view state text ("cold context 101.1k 12m 13s") drawn in the
  right group was noticed as not matching the described layout.

## Owner rulings: topbar contract and account cell (2026-09-13, owner)

- The topbar schema and organization are FIXED, no exceptions. The
  whole-view hibernated and cold-gate states are reversed; cells that lack
  a session fact are optional by presence and draw empty in place, and the
  context chip and warning strip are always present.
- The title is the workspace SUMMARY from the vendor's `ai-title` line
  when present, else the name.
- Clicking the account cell presents a dropdown of the login options (every
  known account root, the current one marked); one option is presented as
  a one-row dropdown.
- The gns-cowork plugin's PowerShell SessionStart hooks (unguarded on macOS
  in 9.10.0, the latest) were stripped locally from the installed copy with
  a backup beside it; upstream still carries them.
## Judgement calls for the topbar layout (2026-09-13)

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | Which distance the ruling's single edge/gap token takes, the row's old 0.75rem inter-group gap or the groups' own 0.5rem cell gap | The CELL gap, 0.5rem, as `--topbar-cell-gap`, used for the row's padding AND every gap in the strip | The ruling words it as "edge padding equal to the padding between cells", and the cell gap is the distance between two cells; the row's 0.75rem was the distance between GROUPS, which the ruling does not name | Give `.topbar-row` its own gap again and leave the token to the groups |
| 2026-09-13 | Which mechanism centers the title on the full strip, the three-track grid or an absolutely centered title layer | The grid, `1fr minmax(0, auto) 1fr` | Absolute centering cannot know either group's width, so it can only guess a max-width and can still overlap an asymmetric strip, where the tracks make non-overlap a fact of the layout; the reveal layer is the row's sibling in the host, so neither choice disturbs it | Give `.topbar-title` `position: absolute; left: 50%` and drop the tracks |
| 2026-09-13 | What gives when the title is wider than the free space between the groups | The TITLE, by ellipsis; the groups keep their content width | A clipped title still says which workspace this is and carries the whole text in its tooltip, where a squeezed group clips controls that are clicked | Give the outer tracks a `minmax(0, 1fr)` and let the groups shrink |
| 2026-09-13 | Where the session-line reveal anchors once the glyph and the label are one cell | On the CELL | The ruling's reason for the pairing is that the two are one element; anchoring under the label alone would place the panel under half of it and leave a click on the glyph answering nothing | Pass the account button back to `bindSessionReveal` |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether the hibernated and cold-gate cells needed a style of their own to read as the right group's content | No — the right group's own box, at the strip's right edge, is the whole answer | Both already carried the right cells' padding, border and radius; what made them read as a spill was the flex row's centered title, which the tracks fix, so a style of their own would have been a second answer to a question the box already answers | Give `.topbar-hibernated` / `.topbar-cold-gate` their own box |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether the topbar proto's header sketch is regenerated after a comment-only edit | Yes, `make -C proto all` | The committed bindings carry the file's leading comment verbatim and `check-generated` diffs a fresh regen byte for byte, so a comment-only edit that skipped codegen would fail that gate | Strip leading comments from the generated headers |

## Owner ruling: every daemon update reaches the footer (2026-09-13, owner)

Every update the daemon produces for a workspace must reach the user in
some form through the footer (or the feed). The topology is to be
established by an audit: every response vector from the daemon to the
webapp (unary refusals, stream endings, transport failures, warnings,
errors, progress) that is NOT reflected in the footer or the feed today
is catalogued in a summary doc, then remediated.

## Owner ruling: one token-count format (2026-09-13, owner)

Every token count the footer shows (text mostly composed in the daemon)
uses one standardized format: thousands with a `k` suffix and decimals,
e.g. `10.1k`, never `10,113`.

### Sweep and judgement calls (2026-09-13, footer/token-format)

Swept every footer token-count site (`daemon/internal/resolve/footer/*.go`):
the tokens cell, the expanded usage rows (input/cache-read/cache-write/
output/thinking), the alarm sentence, the per-response first-token latency,
the subagent row's running token sum, and the context-budget/rate-limit/
bring-up-failure activity lines. Every site that draws a token COUNT already
routed through the shared `figures.Tokens` formatter
(`daemon/internal/figures/tokens.go`) and its webapp mirror `formatTokens`
(`webapp/src/format.ts`) — no production code needed to change. The
activity-line text that isn't a bare count (context-budget warnings, cold-gate
detail, bring-up failure detail) is vendor/producer prose passed through
verbatim, never a count this ruling governs.

- **Reused, did not add, a second formatter.** `figures.Tokens` (daemon) and
  `formatTokens` (webapp `src/format.ts`) already existed, already matched
  this exact ruling's spelling, and are documented in `webapp/AGENTS.md` as
  the module's ONE token formatter. No site needed rerouting.
- **Rounding is round-half-up at one fractional digit**, inherited from
  `strconv.FormatFloat(..., 'f', 1, 64)` (Go) and `Math.round` (TS) — both already
  in place, not changed here. `1049` -> `1k` (1.049 rounds to 1.0, trimmed);
  `1050` -> `1.1k` (1.05 rounds to 1.1). Added both as explicit boundary tests
  since the ruling names them, alongside `10113` -> `10.1k` and the
  million-exact case `1000000` -> `1M`.
- **Unit promotion is decided on the RENDERED value**, not the raw count —
  already the rule in both formatters (`999950` renders `1M`, not `1000.0k`).
  No change; verified by the existing boundary test plus the newly added ones.

## The shim's producer identity after a refused start, 2026-09-13

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | A `SessionStart` hook blocked the opening AFTER the vendor query was live, so the converter had already written rows under the name the attempt had just announced. Three ways out were open: announce nothing until the name is certain, let the store accept a same-name re-announce, or stop un-naming. Which? | Stop un-naming: when rows were written, the producer name AND the identity file stand, and the retry reuses them | The name is not the attempt's, it is the CONVERSATION's — write ids are derived from it, and the rows that carry it are real history on a real book. Deferring the announcement is not available (the converter writes the moment the query is live, which is before the start is settled), and a same-name re-announce was already idempotent, so the only thing left to fix was the abandonment claiming a name it no longer owned. The engine now ASKS (`Persistence.producerHasWrittenRows`) instead of learning by exception | Restore the unconditional `clearProducer()` + `identityStore.forget()` in `startSession`'s catch (`src/engine/session.ts`) and drop `producerHasWrittenRows` |
| 2026-09-13 | Keeping the name means a `fresh` retry would re-key to a newly minted id. Mint anyway, or reuse? | Reuse the recorded id | A second mint keys the retry's rows to a book the first attempt's rows are not on, splitting one conversation at the failed start — the same split the re-key guard exists to prevent | Drop `recordedOriginalVendorSessionId` from `src/engine/session.ts` |
| 2026-09-13 | A refused StartSession reached no surface. Which fault kind, and does the link go dead as it does for a shim that will not come up? | `resume_failed`, and the link is left alone | `shim_start_failed` and the dead link both name the shim PROCESS, which here is up and answering — what it refused is the SESSION. `resume_failed` already has a typed arm that carries a cause, so nothing new goes on the wire; saying the link died would send every surface hunting a process that is right there | Drop `noteSessionRefused` and the wrapper around `askToStartSession` (`internal/workspace/sessions.go`) |
| 2026-09-13 | File the fault in each refusal arm, or once around the call? | Once around the call | The refusal arms are a growing set and every one of them leaves the workspace with no session; an arm added later must not be able to go unsurfaced by forgetting a call. The two non-failures — a cold gate and a stand-down this daemon ordered — are named exceptions rather than the default | Same as above |
| 2026-09-13 | The cold-gate card DID have a failure branch, and it drew "the daemon could not be reached" over a daemon that answered. Reword only this card, or the rule? | The rule, in `src/rpc/refuse.ts` | The codebase already refuses this exact lie about a malformed view ("it travels up loudly rather than being drawn as 'could not be reached', which would be a lie"); the same reasoning covers a coded answer. `callFailure` is the one place that decides, `drawTransportRefusal` words itself through it when a caller hands it the error, and no call site outside the cold gate had to change | Restore the literal `refusal("transport", …)` in `src/feed/asks/cold-gate.ts` and drop `callFailure` |
## Owner rulings: the topbar's fixed schema and the vendor's title (2026-09-13, owner)

A. **THE TOPBAR SCHEMA AND ORGANIZATION ARE FIXED, NO EXCEPTIONS.** The
whole-view states `hibernated` (tag 11) and `cold_gate` (tag 12) landed
earlier the same day are REVERSED: both fields and both messages are retired
per the file's retired-tag convention. Every cell is always present in the
strip; a cell whose session fact is unknown draws its "no session" content in
its own slot. `model_selector`, `permission_mode_picker` and `fast_mode` are
OPTIONAL BY PRESENCE (absent → the webapp draws a dash in the slot, never
omits the cell); the `context` chip and the `warnings` strip are ALWAYS
present — the chip carries the hibernated or cold context size (0 when
unknown) with the reason in its hover, and the strip carries the state as a
warning line. The daemon's readiness gate publishes once naming and account
are known, always.

B. **THE TITLE IS THE WORKSPACE SUMMARY** when the vendor has one. The vendor
writes an `ai-title` transcript line; the shim decodes it, `conversation.v1`
carries it as `SessionUpdate.title` (`SessionTitle`), and the resolver
composes `TopbarTitle.text` from it in preference to the workspace name.

### Judgement calls implementing the two rulings

| date | question | call | why | rejected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-13 | "OPTIONAL by presence" for three MESSAGE fields, which already have presence in proto3 — document it, or write the `optional` keyword | WRITE THE KEYWORD. `TopbarModelSelector.selected` in the same file already spells an optional message field that way, and the generated Go and TS are byte-identical in shape (a pointer / a `\| undefined`), so the keyword costs nothing and states the ruling where a reader of the schema will look | Leave the fields bare and put "optional by presence" in prose only |
| 2026-09-13 | The ruling puts the session-less state in the warning strip "as a warning line", but every `TopbarWarning` carried a `detail` arm and the webapp refused one without | `TopbarWarning.detail` may now be UNSET: a warning that is a STATEMENT, drawn as a text row with no click | The ruling's line has nothing to reveal, and for the cold gate there is nothing to answer in the strip at all — the feed's gate card is the one place a gate is answered. A new detail arm would have been a second overlay for a fact whose whole content is its sentence | Add a `TopbarSessionlessWarningDetail` arm carrying the same sentence again |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether the vendor's summary replaces the whole composed title or only the workspace NAME half of it | Only the name. The branch suffix and the rule about when it is worth showing are unchanged | The summary answers "which conversation is this" and the branch answers "which checkout"; they are different facts, and the ruling replaces the one the owner called the name | Have the summary replace "name · branch" outright |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether the retired states' log records go with them, leaving the session-less strip unannounced | No — the edge detector is RE-AIMED at the session-scoped half: info when a strip publishes with its controls absent, info when they return | A strip drawn with dashes where its controls belong is still the loudest thing a reader can see, and an invisible action is a logging defect; the record now reads the published view's own `model_selector` rather than a retired field | Drop the two info records with the fields they watched |
| 2026-09-13 | Which figure the session-less context chip states when both a cold gate's count and a remembered `context_usage` exist | The cold gate's | It is what the shim read off the transcript for THIS resume; a remembered usage is what some earlier session last reported | Prefer the remembered usage as the more recent statement |
| 2026-09-13 | Where the shim reads the `ai-title` line, given nothing on the SDK stream announces it and the transcript grows all session | An incremental TAIL from a byte cursor (`engine/title.ts`), read at the two edges the context usage is reported at: session start and turn end | Re-reading a megabyte transcript per turn would make the cost of the title grow with the conversation; the cursor stops at the last newline so a torn record is read next pass, and a shorter file is reread from the beginning because it is a different conversation | Re-read the whole transcript per turn, or read it once at start and never again |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether the title is REPLAYED to a joining WatchSession subscriber | Yes, beside the model, fast mode and account usage | The title is read during StartSession and the daemon opens its standing WatchSession only after StartSession has answered, so without the replay the topbar would draw the workspace name until some later turn happened to change the title | Push it on change only, like the event arms |
| 2026-09-13 | Whether "the warning strip is ALWAYS drawn" retires its own "nothing is drawn when nothing is wrong" rule | No. The FIELD is always set; an EMPTY list still draws no chip | The ruling's subject is the wire — the strip must be there to carry the state line — and the proto's own rule that a control over an empty list only invites the click that proves it is empty was not reopened | Draw a quiet warning chip on every strip |

Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01GEXRT62v8zC9WXtBtZBjp4

## The shim's start path when the vendor ends the opening, 2026-09-13

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | Workspace `2b81f45a724642ef` resumed `bf5fcae1-…` and waited the whole 45s init bound, twice, with the shim logging that "rows were written before the start was refused". What were those rows? | ONE row, upserted twice: `entry.position` 350773, kind `page_line`, book `bf5fcae1-…`, an `AgentHook` for `SessionStart:resume` — started at 18:17:56.565 and SUCCEEDED (exit 0, 12ms) at .779. The second attempt wrote the identical row at 18:18:41.490 | Decoded from a copy of `~/.cache/agent-repl/store/events.db` (`entry.frame`, `protoc --decode_raw`); `convert/hooks.ts:192` puts the hook name in `command`, which is why the frame reads `SessionStart:resume` | Nothing to reverse — a reading of the record |
| 2026-09-13 | Was the vendor silent, or did its query DIE and the shim not notice? | Not determinable from any artifact, and that is itself the defect | `writeQueryDeathTerminal` returns early with no open turn (`engine/session.ts`), `onQueryLost` logged nothing at all, and neither settled the pending start — so a dead query and a silent one produced byte-identical evidence: two log lines 45s apart | Nothing to reverse — the gap is closed by the change below |
| 2026-09-13 | Fix the silence case, or every case that ends a query before `init`? | Every case, at the source | The bound exists for silence; a `result` with `is_error`, an ended stream, an exited child and a throwing iterator are each a conclusive answer already in hand, and sitting out 45s on one lets the daemon's own 60s bound fire and name the shim instead of the vendor | Delete `settleStartOnErrorResult` and the `settleStartOnQueryEnd` call at the top of `onQueryLost` |
| 2026-09-13 | The vendor's own refusal text ("No conversation found with session ID …") never reaches the shim on the message stream. Take it from stderr, or leave the detail as the shim's own observation? | Take it: `Options.stderr` is wired through `QuerySpec.onStderr`, a bounded 2000-char tail is kept, and it is appended to the refusal's `detail` | The CLI prints its reason there and nowhere else; without it a timed-out start can only report the quiet it observed | Drop `onStderr` from `QuerySpec`/`RealQuerySpec` and `withVendorStderr` from the catch block |
| 2026-09-13 | A failed start walked away from the vendor child it had opened. Leave it (out of scope) or close it? | Close it, and guard a released query's loop end | The retry would otherwise be a SECOND child on one conversation — two writers on one transcript — and the closed query's loop end would report `query_died` and a permanent fault for a session that had merely refused a start | Delete the orphan-close block in the catch and `isStaleLoop` |
| 2026-09-13 | Why does THIS session id resume silently while its sibling under the same account does not? | Not answered here, and no shim-side cause was found | The transcript is structurally sound: 86 lines, no `summary`, no compact boundary, a consistent `parentUuid` chain, `cwd` matching the workspace, and a tail that is 12 keep-alive turns and a trailing `mode` record. The vendor emitted its hook and stopped; everything after that is inside the CLI | Nothing to reverse — reopen if the fixed start path reports a vendor `result` or stderr naming a cause |


## Owner ruling: webapp-side failures reach the footer (2026-09-13, owner)

Exception to "the daemon composes, the webapp draws": when the webapp
cannot talk to the daemon it knows that itself and draws it: footer
status DISCONNECTED (detached), substatus "daemon unreachable", and the
activity line an ad-hoc message about the nature/context of the missing
connection. More generally every webapp-side failure gets a footer status
(disconnected, unless the lead argues otherwise), substatus and activity.
## The immediate shutdown's ordinary departures, 2026-09-13

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | Eight `daemon.shimclient.exit` ERROR "shim died" landed during `UpdateShutdownSchedule{now}` with `stand_down_asked=false`, on a client the drain's walk never touched. Arm the latch on more paths, or change what the latch IS? | Change what it is: the latch is the DAEMON's, not only the client's | The measured shape is ONE shim process with TWO clients — the supervisor's spawn record and the fleet's adopted one, because a bring-up whose StartSession refused leaves its shim serving and the next bring-up adopts that inert survivor. No per-client arming can cover a client nothing holds; the question every consumer actually asks is "did this daemon order the departure", and only the supervisor's latch answers it for every client it handed out | Drop `client.daemonStandDown` and restore `StandingDown()` to `c.standDown.Load()` (`daemon/internal/shimclient/client.go`) |
| 2026-09-13 | The supervisor's latch went up inside `StandDownEverySpawn`, which runs AFTER `standEverySessionDown`. Leave it there? | No — `ShutdownNow` raises it first, through `BeginStandDown` | The walk itself causes departures, so a latch raised after the walk is false for every one of them; raised first, the two halves of the shutdown answer the same question the same way | Delete the `BeginStandDown` call in `drain.controller.ShutdownNow` (`daemon/internal/drain/controller.go`) |
| 2026-09-13 | A register arriving during the stand-down cost a spawn WARN, a bring-up ERROR, a register ERROR and a `shim_start_failed` fault. Which refusal arm answers it? | `spawn_failed`, the arm the caller already read, plus the `ErrStandingDown` sentinel | `transferring_away` is a GetFeedPage arm and OpenWorkspaceError has none for a departing daemon, so any other name is a proto question rather than a remediation; the sentinel is what lets the register tell this apart without a new arm, and the transport's answer is unchanged | Delete the `Supervisor.StandingDown()` guard at the top of `Fleet.bringUpClient` (`daemon/internal/workspace/sessions.go`) |
| 2026-09-13 | Is `daemon.shimclient.spawn` "refused a spawn: this daemon is standing down" a warning? | No — INFO | It is the latch doing exactly what it exists for, and the caller gets a typed error to act on; the bring-up now reads the latch first, so reaching the backstop at all is the narrow race between the two reads, which is an ordinary schedule | Restore `log.Warn` in `supervisor.Spawn` (`daemon/internal/shimclient/supervisor.go`) |
| 2026-09-13 | Is `the workspace lock reads free but a shim is listening` the designed recovery (INFO) or the phantom-shim class from realtest 7 (WARN)? | The designed recovery — INFO | The shim takes its conversation locks INSIDE StartSession, so free-and-listening is what an INERT shim looks like by contract; the boot's own copy of the branch has said so at INFO since `TestAnInertSurvivorIsNotWarnedAbout`, and one condition at two levels is drift. A phantom OUTLIVED a lock it had taken; this shim never took one, and all four occurrences followed a `vendor_start_failed` on the very shim being adopted, one bring-up earlier | Restore `log.Warn` at the `inert` branch in `Fleet.bringUpClient` (`daemon/internal/workspace/sessions.go`) |

## The failed start's own shim, 2026-09-13

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | A `Fleet.Start` whose StartSession was refused returned before `f.remember`, so no client held the shim it had spawned -- and the shim kept serving. Stop it, or teach the next bring-up to recognize it? | STOP it: a start that fails leaves no shim of its own serving | The next bring-up recognizing it is what already happened -- it adopted the process this daemon was still supervising, which is one shim with two clients and the disagreement at stand-down that followed. Recognition cannot undo the leak either: the process holds ~95 MiB and the workspace socket for as long as nothing stops it | Delete the `path == pathSpawned` call to `Fleet.stopFailedStart` in `Fleet.Start` (`daemon/internal/workspace/sessions.go`) |
| 2026-09-13 | Does the stop cover a shim the failed start ADOPTED as well as one it spawned? | No -- only the spawned one | An adopted shim was serving before this start and would still be serving without it; ending someone else's survivor because our StartSession refused destroys a conversation this daemon does not own. The `bringUpPath` the bring-up returns is what tells them apart | Widen the guard to every non-nil client in `Fleet.Start` |
| 2026-09-13 | Is the ResumeCold path an orphan too? | No, and it is left alone | Its client is in `f.sessions` -- the park put it there precisely so the gate's answer has somewhere to go -- so a failed re-open leaves a shim the fleet still holds and `Fleet.Stop` still reaches, which is not the orphan this invariant is about. Killing it would also destroy the standing gate the user is being asked to answer | Call `stopFailedStart` from `Fleet.ResumeCold`'s error paths |
| 2026-09-13 | What does the adopt path do when the shim it is about to adopt is one this daemon's own supervisor holds? | Refuse, by name (`ErrAdoptOwnSpawn`), and file the workspace fault | It is a BUG in this daemon rather than a state of the world: two clients on one process disagree about the exit, the redial and the lock, and there is no recovery to attempt. The supervisor's registry is the only witness, because a spawn reaches the session map only after StartSession answers | Delete the `refuseAdoptingOurOwnSpawn` calls in `Fleet.bringUpClient` (`daemon/internal/workspace/sessions.go`) |
| 2026-09-13 | Is the stop's own `KillSession` refusal loud? | INFO when this daemon armed the stand-down, WARN otherwise -- the same rule `verbs.kill` applies | A start that just failed has no session to end, so `no_session` is the ORDINARY answer here; the process stop after it is unconditional and the refusal is evidence, not a defect | Restore an unconditional `log.Warn` in `Fleet.stopFailedStart` (`daemon/internal/workspace/failedstart.go`) |
## The vendor's folder trust and the silent start, 2026-09-13

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | Nothing in the SDK or the CLI offers a trust option. Is writing into the vendor's own `.claude.json` acceptable, or should the shim only report the untrusted directory? | Write it | The vendor itself prescribes this exact key in the only line it emits about the condition ("set projects[\"<dir>\"].hasTrustDialogAccepted: true in <config_root>/.claude.json"), and every agent-repl workspace is a worktree no human will ever open interactively to answer the dialog, so reporting alone leaves every session running with its allowlists dropped forever | Delete the `trusted()` call in `queryFactory` (`agent-shim/claude/shim/src/main.ts`) and `src/trust.ts` |
| 2026-09-13 | Which directory is the trust keyed by for a git worktree? | The MAIN repository | Grounded against the bundled binary: with only the worktree's own path trusted the warning still fires and names the main repository; with the main repository trusted it goes away. Resolved from the worktree's `.git` FILE, never by running git | Return `cwd` unconditionally from `trustRoot` (`agent-shim/claude/shim/src/trust.ts`) |
| 2026-09-13 | An account root that does not exist makes the trust write fail. Refuse the spawn, or run degraded? | Refuse | A missing or unwritable account root is a real fault, and the whole point of this change is that a session must not silently run in a posture nobody chose | Wrap `ensureWorkspaceTrusted` in a try/catch that logs and continues (`agent-shim/claude/shim/src/main.ts`) |
| 2026-09-13 | Three `queryFactory` tests named a fictional `/accounts/primary` account root, which the trust write now cannot use. Amend them? | Amend to a temp directory | The directory was a stand-in for a path those tests never read; what they pin — that the whole spec reaches the real query — is untouched | `agent-shim/claude/shim/test/main.test.ts`, the "forwards the whole spec" describe |
| 2026-09-13 | The brief asked the 45s detail to name the TRUST DIALOG as the likely cause of "hook events then silence". The reproduction disproves that for the pinned binary. What should the detail say? | Name the vendor's init-on-first-turn behavior, and the trust key only as a companion to confirm | An untrusted directory under claude 2.1.220 warns on stderr and runs; it never withholds `init`. A detail that named the wrong cause would send every future reader down the same dead end the lead just walked | `silentStartReason` in `agent-shim/claude/shim/src/engine/session.ts` |

## The client's link verdict, drawing the ruling above, 2026-09-13

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | The ruling says "disconnected unless the lead argues otherwise". Do the two OpenFeed rows the daemon ANSWERED and refused (audit N3 rows 13, 14, 15) draw "daemon unreachable"? | Status disconnected, substatus **"feed not tailing"** | The daemon was reached and answered; the audit already records the opposite wording as a defect of its own ("a card that says the daemon is unreachable when it is not", N3 row 14), and repeating it here would be the same lie in a second place. What actually stopped is the feed's tail, so that is the word | `KIND_SUBSTATUS.feed_not_tailing` in `webapp/src/rpc/link.ts` |
| 2026-09-13 | Same question for the `frame_undecodable` card (N2 row 11) | Status disconnected, substatus **"frame unreadable"** | One frame could not be read; the link is up and the next frame draws. Naming the daemon unreachable would be contradicted by the screen a second later | `KIND_SUBSTATUS.frame_undecodable_card` in the same table |
| 2026-09-13 | What LIFTS a verdict? A push is the obvious candidate | Only evidence the link works: a unary the daemon ANSWERED (success or refusal), or a stream that read a frame after having ended. A push does not lift one | The footer's stream and the verb that failed are different calls -- the ruling's own incident is a footer that kept pushing `parked` while an `AnswerColdGate` never landed. Lifting on a push would restore exactly that | `clearClientFailures()` in `callUnary` and in `watchStream`'s recovery block |
| 2026-09-13 | A frame that DECODES: does it lift every verdict, or only the undecodable one? | Only the undecodable one, through a narrow `clearClientFailure(kind)` | A decoded frame disproves "the last frame could not be read" and nothing else; it says nothing about a verb that never arrived. Clearing everything would put the footer back under a link a click cannot reach | `clearClientFailure("frame_undecodable_card")` in `watchStream`'s `consume` |
| 2026-09-13 | Does a client verdict close the composer, as a pushed `disconnected` does under R7? | No | R7 closes it because the DAEMON says the session is gone. A client verdict says the last thing this page sent did not arrive, and the only thing that lifts it is the user sending something that does -- closing the composer takes away the retry the strip's own line invites (it also broke `composer.integration` duplicate-submission, which retries exactly that way) | Republish `"disconnected"` from `publishStatus` in `webapp/src/footer/footer.ts` |
| 2026-09-13 | Does the client-composed strip replace the whole dock? | Only the three status cells. The clock, the tokens and the chips keep coming from the daemon's LAST pushed strip, and the expanded panel still opens | A link that just died does not make the last figures untrue, it makes them the last ones; blanking half the dock would say more than the client knows | The `last` argument of `drawClientDisconnectedStrip` (`webapp/src/footer/strip.ts`) |
| 2026-09-13 | The feed's refused-`OpenFeed` verdict is replaced within the same tick by the tail's own ending verdict, so the drawn line reads "WatchFeed stream ended (producer_ended)". Chase it? | No, not here. The report lands and is logged at info; the drawn line is the tail's | A generator that returned is an ending `watchStream` cannot tell from a dead link, so naming it correctly means teaching the retry loop the difference -- audit priority 6, and a change to the standing-stream contract rather than to this wiring | Nothing to reverse; the report stands at `feed.ts`'s error arm |
| 2026-09-13 | A redraw triggered by a verdict can hit a last view that is malformed, and the caller is an rpc | Log it, file `frame_undecodable`, skip the redraw -- exactly what `watchStream` does with an unreadable push | Letting it throw makes the rpc's own answer read as a transport failure at its call site, which is the misnaming this whole piece exists to remove | The catch in the `onClientVerdict` subscription (`webapp/src/footer/footer.ts`) |

Wire the latch on one more path, or make it unforgettable? | Unforgettable: `daemonStandDown` is a parameter of `newClient` (`daemon/internal/shimclient/client.go`), not a field each construction site remembers to assign afterwards | Both supervisor sites did assign it, so the defect the assignment shape invites is exactly the one that cannot be found by reading the two sites that happen to be right; a constructor argument makes a latchless client impossible to build rather than merely unobserved | Restore the `daemonStandDown func() bool` field assignment at `supervisor.Spawn` and `supervisor.Adopt` and drop the parameter |
