## A file that vanished after everything in it was read, 2026-09-13

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | The `tree_removed` rule did not cover a reaped task spool or a cleaned-up transcript under a STANDING directory, both of which the reader had already read to the end. Widen the rule, or leave them WARN? | Widen it: `reason` grows `ended` and `fully_read` | The WARNING exists for bytes that could have been lost, and a file whose every byte was read and committed lost none; the vendor reaping a task's output file after the task ends is the ordinary shape of that path, not something an operator can act on | Return only `reasonTreeRemoved` from `vanishReason` (`agent-shim/claude/shim-sidecar/cycle.go`) |
| 2026-09-13 | "Fully read" needs a size to compare the committed offset against. The size at the vanish is unknowable; use the last one a poll observed, or drop the arm? | The last size a SUCCESSFUL poll observed, and only when a poll ever observed one | It is the only size the reader has evidence for, and the pair it is compared against -- the committed offset -- moves only on a store ack, so a batch still in flight, held, or mid-tail keeps the file's bytes outstanding and keeps the WARNING | `LastSize` and the `sized` flag (`agent-shim/claude/shim-sidecar/internal/tail/tailer.go`) |
| 2026-09-13 | The tracker carried the tree removal as a boolean through to the conclusion. Add two more booleans, or name the reason? | Name it: `Lost.BenignEnd` is a string, `""` = a genuine loss | Three ways of ending the same way is a vocabulary, and three booleans would let two of them be true at once; the conclusion's own sentence can then say WHICH, and the wire arm stays `file_vanished` exactly as before | `Lost.TreeRemoved` as a bool and the `treeRemoved` entry field (`agent-shim/claude/shim-sidecar/internal/stale/stale.go`) |

## Owner rulings, evening batch (2026-09-13, owner)

- Realtest 9 (and the Conversation section) is the lead's to author, run
  and remediate to completion without the owner's intervention. No UI
  look-and-feel changes unless corrective.
- Every daemon fault kind reaches the footer. Proto additions and modest
  changes are pre-approved for it. Reuse an existing footer STATUS where
  one fits (never invent a status when one exists); substatuses are
  concise, well-formed buckets forming an ONTO relation over the activity
  values (several activities may land in one substatus; substatuses never
  overfit to activity cardinality). Status is most general, substatus less
  general, activity least general.
- The shim start contract: the lead's recommendation is taken (settle the
  start on a proven-live control round-trip; learn the init facts with the
  first turn), since the owner wants the section brought home without
  intervention.
- test-all runs as the section gate afterwards.

## The fault partition, 2026-09-13

The owner's evening ruling pre-approved proto additions and modest changes for
"every daemon fault kind reaches the footer", and asked that the real partition
be derived from the kinds' semantics rather than copied from the ruling's
worked example. These are the calls that derivation took.

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | The ruling's example put `shim_reported`, `classifier_failed`, `bounce_unknown` and `daemon_state_unreadable` under `disconnected · degraded`. Take the example, or derive? | Derive: the first three do not escalate the status at all, and `daemon_state_unreadable` goes to `blocked · daemon_impaired` | The shim ANSWERED in all three of the first: a shim that pushed a diagnostic is alive, a classifier run is a headless side errand, an undetermined bounce disposition is an accounting question for a human. And it is not a wording question — `disconnected` closes the webapp's composer (`webapp/src/main.ts`), so escalating them would withhold the prompt from a session that is serving perfectly | Move the four rows in `sessionFaultCells` (`daemon/internal/health/footer.go`) to `{FaultStatusDisconnected, FaultSubStatusDegraded}` and add that bucket to `disconnectedByFault` |
| 2026-09-13 | The example also put `conversation_abandoned` under `disconnected · dead`. | Non-escalating, activity line only | It is the record of what a SUCCESSFUL fresh bring-up left behind — the workspace has a LIVE session, which `kinds.go` says in its own comment — and nothing closes it, so `dead` would park a healthy workspace on a permanent disconnected strip with the composer shut | Same site: give it a `{FaultStatusDisconnected, FaultSubStatusDead}` cell |
| 2026-09-13 | A fault could reach the footer as its own activity arm per family, or as one shared arm. | One shared `FooterStatusActivityFault{kind, detail}`, with `shim_start_failed` keeping its existing richer leaf | Rule 3 makes activity the least general and admits one kind serving several families where the payloads do not differ; every kind but `shim_start_failed` carries exactly a name and a line, and `shim_start_failed` differs because it also counts the held prompts the failure dropped | Split the leaf per family and widen each activity oneof |
| 2026-09-13 | `fault` is a status-INDEPENDENT activity kind, so it needed a precedence. | Below the status-bound kinds that explain their own step, above `notification` | A cell that exists to explain its substatus cannot be crowded out of it (the wakeup countdown under waiting, the injected item under loading); but a notification from before the fault must not hide the condition the user has to be told about, which is the exception `start_failed` already held | Move the `faultLine` branch in `daemon/internal/resolve/footer/activity.go` below the `notificationLine` branch in each of the ten resolvers |
| 2026-09-13 | The footer could be told about faults at each raise site, or from one hook. | One hook: `health.ObserveFaults` decorates the state client every raise site shares | Nine sites open faults, four of them outside the health reporter entirely; per-site plumbing is exactly how three kinds came to have a footer path and sixteen did not | Delete the decorator from `cmd/claude-repld/graph.go` and call `Footer.OpenFault` beside each raise |
| 2026-09-13 | A fault claims a status; so does the live link state. Which wins the disconnected step? | The link state | It is the live truth about the link and the fault is the standing record beside it; a fault only claims the step where the link state claimed none | Consult `standingFault` before the link switch in `disconnected` (`daemon/internal/resolve/footer/status.go`) |
| 2026-09-13 | A daemon-scoped fault belongs to no workspace. Draw it nowhere, or everywhere? | On every workspace's strip | It is every workspace that is owed the service the daemon cannot give; a `prompts_dir_missing` that no strip mentions is the same silence the ruling was about | Drop `mutateAll` and the `daemonFaults` set (`daemon/internal/resolve/footer/faults.go`) |
| 2026-09-13 | Standing faults recorded by a PREVIOUS daemon are in the state store at boot and no strip learns about them. | Out of scope; the hook covers open and close only, as the ruling words it | The ruling names the open/close hooks; seeding at boot is a separate question about which workspaces should be republished and when, and guessing at it would put stale faults on strips with no retraction path | Read `OpenFaults` per workspace at registration and replay them into `Footer.OpenFault` |
## Authoring realtest 9: the edges a turn does not write, 2026-09-13

`TestRealtestSendAPrompt` was authored against
docs/REALTEST-PLAN.md item 9 ("Send a prompt, watch it think, read the
answer. Tab arm, footer and feed through the whole turn."). Four of the
facts that item names have NO log edge behind them, so the realtest
asserts what exists and records each gap here as a LOGGING DEFECT for the
lead to dispatch. No product change was made while authoring it.

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | Item 9 asks that "the prompt reaches the daemon (`daemon.promptqueue.submit` record)" be asserted. On the ordinary path the queue's own door writes only DEBUG ("no lease stands; the submission takes the ordinary path", `daemon/internal/promptqueue/submit.go`) and the deployed daemon runs at the contract's INFO default, so no submit record is persisted. Assert on a record that is not written, or on something else? | Assert on the daemon's own durable `turns` row and on `daemon.promptqueue.deliver` (INFO); REPORT whether the submit record was there | A gate on a record the deployed level does not persist would fail every run for a reason that is not the product's, and the two edges chosen are both durable and both strictly stronger: the row is the daemon having STORED the prompt and the delivery is it having handed it on. LOGGING DEFECT: the submit door has no INFO record, so nothing in a default-level log says a submission was accepted at the queue — only that one was delivered, which is a different moment and does not exist for a held or refused one | Turn `rt9ReportSubmitDoor` into an assertion once the door records at INFO (`e2e/realtest/realtest_9_send_a_prompt_test.go`) |
| 2026-09-13 | Item 9 asks that "the footer status goes idle→thinking→(sub-statuses submitting/thinking)→idle/done" be observed. The progress footer is drawn in the webview from `daemon/internal/resolve/footer`, whose only account of itself is `daemon.footer.status_decision` at DEBUG; elisp cannot read the webview and no INFO record states the phase word. Assert it how? | Assert the same status vocabulary where it IS readable — the tab arm — and say in the manifest that the footer itself was not read | The arm and the footer status are the daemon's ONE lifecycle coarsened onto two surfaces (`:submitting`/`:thinking` on the tab, `thinking · submitting` in the strip), so the arm is evidence about the same resolution; it is not evidence about the strip's own composition, and the run says so rather than implying it did read the footer. LOGGING DEFECT: no INFO record states the footer's resolved status, so a run can neither assert it nor reconstruct it from a log afterwards | Assert on the footer's own record once it exists, in `rt9AssertTheArm`'s neighbourhood |
| 2026-09-13 | Nothing records the TAB ARM's transitions either: `elisp.status.tab-state` is log-verbose (`lisp/status.el`). Sample the arm and assert every transition, or only what is deterministic? | Sample it (`rt9ArmWatch`), ASSERT the settled arm, REPORT the working arms | The settled arm after a concluded turn is a state and asserting it is stable; a transient the fake vendor may pass through in under one sample interval is a race, and the repo's standing rule is that a rerun is not a strategy — a sampler that failed on a missed transition would be a flake, not a finding. LOGGING DEFECT: the arm's transitions are unrecorded, so the harness cannot tell "the arm never changed" from "the change was shorter than the sample" | Assert `sawWorking` in `rt9AssertTheArm` once the arm's transitions are recorded at DEBUG or above |
| 2026-09-13 | Item 9 asks that "the answer text is in the feed" be asserted. No log carries a feed row's prose: the webapp's draw records carry character counts and row ids, the daemon's feed resolver logs only exception shapes, and the daemon's durable tables hold the prompt and not the response. The store's `entry` frames do carry it, but the owner's `events.db` is 790MB and this layer's one read path snapshots and `quick_check`s a copy per read. Assert it how? | Two halves that meet: `feed.final-answer-marked` (the feed marked an answering row for this turn) and a `feed.draw-text-block` whose `characters` equals the length of the fake scenario's known opening sentence | The mark is the feed's own statement that the answer is drawn in it, and the length ties a drawn block to a known text without the log carrying text. The store was rejected on cost, not on principle: snapshotting 790MB per read would put minutes into a run whose acts are seconds, and the repo's bounds rule would then be met by a ceiling nobody could justify. LOGGING DEFECT: no log anywhere carries a feed row's text, so "the right answer reached the feed" is not directly assertable | Assert the text itself once a record carries it; drop the length check then, since it is a stand-in for exactly that |
| 2026-09-13 | The prompt has to reach the composer. Every other act realtest supplies a command's text through the probe transport; realtest 9's act is the send, and `agent-repl-send` reads the composer rather than taking an argument. Type it as real keys, or insert it through a probe? | TYPE IT, one real key event per character (`rt9TypePrompt`) | A prompt inserted through a probe would test the send with the composer — half of what item 9 names — removed from it. The cost is a keycode table for the prompt's characters and ~30 posts; the prompt is therefore lowercase letters and spaces only, and the composer is READ BACK so a dropped character is a stated harness finding rather than a silent difference between what was typed and what was judged | Replace the per-character presses with one `insert` through the probe transport, and say so in the test's deviations |
| 2026-09-13 | `bin/test-realtest.sh`'s "an unknown realtest selector DECLINES" used `9` as its unknown selector, and 9 is now a realtest. Amend the test? | Amend the FIXTURE, to `99` | The test's subject is an unknown selector and its assertion is untouched; what changed is that its stand-in stopped being unknown. A fixture spelled "the next realtest" stops testing anything the day that realtest lands, so it moves to a number the plan (24 realtests) will never hold | `bin/test-realtest.sh`, `test_unknown_selector_declines_before_anything` |
## The shim's start contract, live signal (2026-09-13)

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | Which control call is the proven-live signal? | `supportedModels()` | The opening owes that call anyway — its answer IS `SessionStarted.model_catalog` — so the proof costs no extra round-trip and the catalog is in hand before the opening returns; the alternatives (`supportedCommands`, `setPermissionMode`) would each be a call spent purely on the proof | Point `proveLive` at another verb and restore the `pushModelCatalog()` call in `startSession`'s success path (`agent-shim/claude/shim/src/engine/session.ts`) |
| 2026-09-13 | The proto REQUIRES `SessionRuntime.agent_binary_version`, which used to come from init. Change the proto, or find another source? | Neither: the SDK's bundled manifest already states it | `runtimeIdentity()` has read the manifest since before this change and init merely overwrote it, so the opening was never actually dependent on init for the field; init's `claude_code_version` still wins when it lands, since the running binary outranks the packaged declaration | Nothing to reverse; if a build ships with no manifest, `requireSessionRuntime` refuses and the start fails saying so, rather than sending a sentinel |
| 2026-09-13 | `agent_binary_version` has no `SessionUpdate` arm, so a late init that CORRECTS it can only be recorded. Add an arm? | No — record and log it | The field is a diagnosis carried once on the opening, not a live chip any surface draws, and the two sources disagree only when a host runs a CLI other than the one the SDK packaged; an arm nothing renders is proto surface with no consumer | Add a `SessionUpdate` arm for it and push from `noteIdentityFacts` |
| 2026-09-13 | A fresh start naming no model now answers with `effective_model` empty, because only init knows the SDK's default. Invent a name, or state absence? | State absence | The ruling says the topbar draws its no-session dashes for the cells init has not filled yet, and a name nothing chose would reach the picker, the record and every later authoritative field; the first turn's init pushes `model_changed` the moment it knows | `noteReportedModel` in `session.ts`, and the two `StartSession, fresh` tests that pin the empty name and the later push |
| 2026-09-13 | Should the mocked vendor's DEFAULT init timing become `after-first-turn`, the real vendor's shape? | No — `at-start` stays the default, with `after-first-turn` as a lever | Every captured corpus and every scenario was recorded with init leading, so flipping the default would re-record the suite instead of testing the contract; the lever reaches the real shape in the tests that are about it, including the Go e2e | Flip the default in `initTiming()` (`agent-shim/claude/shim/src/fake/index.ts`) |
| 2026-09-13 | Nineteen suites settled their starts on `init` and now race the live signal instead. Amend them? | Amend: each HOLDS the live signal (`ScriptedQuery.holdModels`) so it still asserts its own arm | The behavior they pin — a blocking hook, an error result, a stream that ends, the silence bound — is unchanged; what changed is that a scripted query answers the round-trip in a microtask, so without the hold each would assert a race rather than its subject | `holdLiveSignal` / `holdFirstStart` in `test/engine/session.test.ts`, and `holdModels`/`releaseModels` in `test/engine/fakes.ts` |
| 2026-09-13 | `TestStartSessionVendorStartFailedRecovers` was already red on master: `start-once` counts refusals per PROCESS, and the daemon now stops the shim of a failed start, so the retry's new process refuses again. Report it, or fix it? | Fix it, by scoping the one refusal to the account root with a marker file | The lever's own documented purpose is the RECOVERY half of the arm, which became unreachable the moment the daemon started stopping failed shims; leaving it red would mean landing on a suite that is red for a reason this change's own area owns | Restore the `startOnceRefusals` module counter in `agent-shim/claude/shim/src/fake/index.ts` |

## Topbar true center (2026-09-13)

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-13 | The owner reported: "the topbar format is wrong. it's centered on the center section plus the chips. like this: `<login>      <center><chips>            ` but the CENTER and ONLY the center should be in the center. like this: `<login>          <center>         <chips>`". The cause is the row's own template: `grid-template-columns: 1fr minmax(0, auto) 1fr` is `minmax(auto, 1fr)` twice, and an `auto` floor is the track's min-content, so whenever the free share fell below what the right group wanted, the right track was floored at its content while the left one kept the share -- unequal flanks, title pushed left by half the difference. Equalize the tracks, or absolutely position the title? | Equalize the tracks: `minmax(0, 1fr) fit-content(50%) minmax(0, 1fr)`, with `min-width: 0` on both flank groups | Floored at zero, the two free shares are equal by construction at every width, so the title's midpoint IS the row's midpoint -- a fact of the layout rather than a guess. An absolutely centered title cannot know either group's width, so it can only guess a max-width and would still collide on an asymmetric strip. The `fit-content(50%)` cap is the other half of the correction: a content-sized middle track is sized BEFORE the free ones, so an unbounded title would take the strip and leave the groups nothing; capped at half the row's content box it ellipsis-clips and each flank keeps a quarter as a floor | Restore `grid-template-columns: 1fr minmax(0, auto) 1fr` and drop the two `min-width: 0` declarations (`webapp/src/styles.css`, `.topbar-row` / `.topbar-left` / `.topbar-right`) |
| 2026-09-14 | The owner repeated the same ask against the SHIPPED equal-track strip: the title still read `<login>      <center><chips>`. Measuring the live DOM in the owner's webview: row 423..1513 (midpoint 968), tracks 292.5 / 472.9 / 292.5, title box 732..1205 (midpoint 968 -- the TRACK is centered exactly as designed), left group 431..590 (160px wide, leaving 132px of its track empty), right group 1213..1505 (filling its track). The long title ("DWC/chess960-review-failures-enm - DWC/c...") fills its whole 473px box, so its right edge sits one gap from the chips while clear room stands on the left. Redo the grid, or cap the title from the measured flanks? | Cap it: after every draw, an inline `max-width` of `rowWidth - 2 * edgePadding - 2 * max(leftWidth, rightWidth) - 2 * gap` on `.topbar-title` (the owner's strip: 1090 - 16 - 584 - 16 = 474px) | The grid is doing all a grid CAN do -- it centers the track, and a track's occupants are not its business -- so the remaining fact (what the two GROUPS measure inside their equal tracks) exists only after layout and only JS can read it. The cap is measured, not guessed, and EVERY term the row spends is in it -- the strip's own edge inset at each end, then a flank group, then a track gap, before the title's box begins, so the symmetry is exact rather than one inset per side out: the widest box that leaves the same clear space on both sides whichever flank is wider, so the visible text is symmetric about the strip's midpoint and touches neither flank. The grid is left exactly as it is, so a page with no JS running still gets today's strip; the title keeps `text-align: center`, its ellipsis and its tooltip; a hidden panel measures zero and is skipped at DEBUG with nothing at INFO or WARN | Delete `webapp/src/topbar/title-cap.ts` with its suite, and drop the `capTopbarTitle` call and the `watchTitleCap` handle from `webapp/src/topbar/topbar.ts` |
| 2026-09-14 | The owner's live strip read `DWC/chess960-review-failures-enm · DWC/chess960-review-failures-enm` — the same name twice, filling the measured title cap. `resolve/topbar.title()` drops the branch only when it equals the repository's DEFAULT branch, so every worktree workspace whose branch is minted from its name prints its name twice. Widen the cap, or drop the branch? | Drop it: `title()` returns the name alone when `branch == name`, compared against the name ACTUALLY SHOWN (after the session-title and naming fallbacks) | The branch earns its place by saying something the title does not, and a branch spelled exactly like the text beside it says nothing — exactly as the default branch says nothing, which is the rule already in force. Comparing against the shown name rather than the slug keeps the branch visible when the vendor's summary is what is drawn, because there the branch IS a second fact. The cap is not the defect and is left alone | Restore `if branch == "" || branch == s.naming.DefaultBranch` in `daemon/internal/resolve/topbar/resolver.go` and drop `TestTheTitleDropsABranchThatSaysNothing` from `resolver_test.go` |
| 2026-09-14 | The owner ruled: "I want a reasonable, and tight spacing between chips, and ALL extra space to be around the center title section." One token, `--topbar-cell-gap: 0.5rem`, served three distances -- the row's edge inset, the row's track gap, and the gap between chips inside each flank group -- so chips sat 8px apart with no way to tighten them without also pulling the strip off the window edges. Shrink the one token, or split it? | Split it: a second token `--topbar-chip-gap: 0.3rem` used ONLY for `.topbar-left` / `.topbar-right` (and the account cell's half-gap, which lives inside a chip group), with the edge inset and the track gap left on `--topbar-cell-gap` | The ruling names two distances with opposite intents -- chips tight, title flanks generous -- and a single token cannot move in two directions; shrinking it would have pulled the first and last chips toward the window edges, which ruling 1 of 2026-09-13 settled the other way. Each chip the flanks give back widens the title cap by the same amount, since `title-cap.ts` measures `max(leftWidth, rightWidth)` off the drawn groups, so the extra space lands exactly where the ruling asked. The cap code reads the ROW's computed column gap and `padding-left`, both still `--topbar-cell-gap`, so it is untouched | Point `.topbar-left` / `.topbar-right` / `.topbar-account-cell` back at `--topbar-cell-gap` and delete the `--topbar-chip-gap` token (`webapp/src/styles.css`) |
| 2026-09-14 | The owner's screenshot showed the entire strip packed into the left half of the window. Measured in the live webview: `header#topbar` spans x 423 to 2036, but its child `.topbar-row` spans only 423 to 1009 — the header is `display: flex` and its one in-flow child is a flex item with no width rule, so it shrink-wraps to its content, the grid's two `minmax(0, 1fr)` flanks share only the content width, the title is centered inside that shrunken row, and the chips sit near the middle of the strip instead of at its right edge. EVERY EARLIER CENTERING MEASUREMENT WAS TAKEN INSIDE THE SHRINK-WRAPPED ROW. Make the header `display: block`, or make the row fill it? | Make the row fill it: `.topbar-strip` (the wrapper `mountTopbar` inserts, and the header's only in-flow child — the reveal layer is `position: absolute`) takes `flex: 1 1 0; min-width: 0`, and `.topbar-row` takes `width: 100%; min-width: 0` | The header's `display: flex`, `align-items: center` and `gap` are load-bearing declarations with comments that describe them, and switching to `display: block` would leave all three inert and their comments untrue. Growing the flex item is the smaller change and keeps every existing declaration honest. The `.topbar-row` selector named in the brief is NOT a direct child of `#topbar` — `.topbar-strip` is, and it carried no rule at all — so the grow goes on the strip and the row's `width: 100%` is what carries it through; both are pinned by unit cases so a future edit cannot shrink-wrap it again. No token, color, font or order changed | Delete the `.topbar-strip` rule and the `width: 100%` / `min-width: 0` declarations on `.topbar-row` (`webapp/src/styles.css`), and drop the two spanning cases from `webapp/test/topbar/topbar.test.ts` |
| 2026-09-13 | The unit suite's geometry model pinned the OLD tracks -- "the groups never shrink, the title clips" -- which is the behavior the report calls wrong. Amend it? | Amend, and add the defect itself as a case | The model exists to state what the declared template does, and it now states something the app no longer declares; the equal-flanks case and the wide-right-group case are both pinned, so the defect cannot come back unnoticed | `webapp/test/topbar/topbar.test.ts`, "the strip's geometry" |
| 2026-09-14 | The owner ruled, superseding every "true center" row above: "the whitespace/padding on the left and right of the center text should be equal, which is subtly different than it being centered. Currently it is centered in the topbar's full span, but it'd be better if it was centered in the space between the login section (left) and the chips section (right)." Keep the equal free tracks plus the measured cap, or let the flanks size themselves and give the title what is between them? | Let the flanks size themselves: `grid-template-columns: auto minmax(0, 1fr) auto`, with `.topbar-title` keeping `text-align: center`, `min-width: 0` and its ellipsis, and `webapp/src/topbar/title-cap.ts` DELETED with its `capTopbarTitle` call, its `watchTitleCap` handle and its suite | The ruling is about clear SPACE, not a shared midpoint, and a `1fr` track between two `auto` flanks is exactly that space: the text centered inside it has the same slack on each side by construction, at every width, whatever the two groups measure. That makes the fact the measured cap was computing a fact of the layout instead, so the cap — and its resize watcher, its floor and its injected metrics — is dead code and is removed rather than left to drift. A long title still cannot reach a group (a `1fr` track takes only what the `auto` flanks left) and ellipsizes symmetrically, because the box it clips in IS the space between them. The rows above marking the strip "true centered" on the whole span are SUPERSEDED: the title's midpoint is deliberately no longer the strip's midpoint when the groups differ. `.topbar-strip` / `.topbar-row` `width: 100%` from the spanning fix stay | Restore `minmax(0, 1fr) fit-content(50%) minmax(0, 1fr)` on `.topbar-row` and `webapp/src/topbar/title-cap.ts` with its installation in `topbar.ts` and its suite (`webapp/src/styles.css`, `webapp/test/topbar/topbar.test.ts`, `webapp/test/integration/topbar.integration.test.ts`) |

## Sidecar workspace attribution across re-registration (2026-09-14)

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-14 | rt-run39: realtest 7 registered a scratch directory as workspace `af24557b1ddd4b9c` at 00:11:57 and forgot it; realtest 9 registered THE SAME directory as `54578ede3d834dea` at 00:12:25. From 00:12:33 every file-scoped diagnostic that directory produced was addressed to the dead id and refused (`workspace "af24557b1ddd4b9c" is no longer registered`, `forward_undelivered: true`). `daemonclient` cached one (address, dir) → ref and re-read the roster only when the cached DIR differed, so nothing could ever move it. Subscribe to the roster for the life of the process, or bound the cache? | Bound it, as one invariant — A DIRECTORY'S WORKSPACE REF IS ONLY EVER USED WHILE THE ROSTER STILL HOLDS IT: the cache is a map keyed per normalized directory, every entry carries the instant its roster was delivered, an entry older than one poll interval is re-read before it is used, a daemon address change drops the whole map, and a `no longer registered` refusal expires that directory's entry, re-resolves once and retries the forward once before reporting `forward_undelivered` | A standing subscription is a second long-lived stream inside the process whose own logging runs through it, and the roster read it would replace already costs one loopback stream open per miss — sub-millisecond, now capped at one per directory per poll interval. The refusal retry is what closes the window the bound leaves open: the daemon's own classification is the only push this cache will ever get, and acting on it costs one roster read on exactly the records that would otherwise be lost. The replacement is stated ONCE at `info` (`workspace-ref-replaced`, the two ids in the message) and nothing at `warn`: a workspace closed and re-created over the same path is ordinary, and the existing `ErrForwardWorkspaceUnresolvable` refusal still stands for the directory the roster genuinely no longer holds. THE ENTRY-ATTRIBUTION HALF OF THE BRIEF NEEDED NO CHANGE, and this is the finding: `tail.Context.WorkspaceID` (and so `handler.attribute`, and the `workspace_id: 3670dcae` on the `tail-pickup` record) is the SHARED DIRECTORY DIGEST, `md5hex(dir)[:8]`, derived in `internal/discover.ResolveWorkspace` — it is a function of the path and cannot go stale across re-registration, and `store.v1` carries no workspace field at all, so no store row ever held the dead id. Only the ClientLog destination did | Restore the single `cachedAddress`/`cachedDir`/`cachedRef` slot and `invalidateWorkspace(address, id)` in `internal/daemonclient/client.go`, drop `deliver`'s `departed` return with the retry in `Forward`, and delete the six roster-movement subjects in `client_test.go` plus the two `refReplacedObserver` subjects in `main_test.go` |

| 1 | Realtest 10 ("Interrupt a running turn") is NOT authored until the owner names the real-key path. The fake's `!hold` scenario (a turn that stays in flight until an interrupt) is ready for whichever path is chosen. | The owner names the path; the lead authors realtest 10 the same day. |

## Sidebar hover panel (2026-09-14)

| Date | Question | Decision | Why | How to reverse |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-14 | The owner ruled: "in the sidebar, when you hover a workspace, the chevron appears. There should be no chevron. Hovering the workspace should have the same effect as opening the chevron currently does. No need for a chevron to show the dropdown on hover." Open on the bare `mouseenter`/`mouseleave`, or on an intent delay with a travel grace? | Delay both edges: the row opens its panel `HOVER_OPEN_DELAY_MS` (150ms) after the pointer arrives and closes it `HOVER_CLOSE_GRACE_MS` (120ms) after the pointer has left BOTH the row and the panel; keyboard focus opens the same panel at once, with the same grace on the way out. The `.chev` element, its CSS and its `user-select` allowlist entry are deleted; the panel's contents, `clampReveal` placement and look are untouched, the row's click is still `SelectWorkspace`, and the open/close log record is the same `sidebar.row.detail-toggle` operation with a `via` of `hover`, `focus` or `leave` | A pointer crossing the rail on its way elsewhere passes over several rows in a few milliseconds, and a bare `mouseenter` flickers a panel under each of them; the panel hangs BELOW the row rather than touching it, so the pointer is over neither on the way in and a bare `mouseleave` would snatch it away as it is being reached for. Keyboard focus needs no intent delay because a focus move is already deliberate, and it gets the panel at all so the ruling does not cost the rail its accessibility | Restore `drawExpandChevron` and its `line.appendChild` in `webapp/src/sidebar/row.ts`, the `.chev` rules in `webapp/src/styles.css`, and the allowlist entry in `webapp/test/styles.test.ts` |
## Bubble geometry (2026-09-14)

Owner ruling, quoted: "make the max width of response bubbles about 10% wider,
and also make the max response bubble length about 10% longer. Also ensure the
scrollbar of the response bubble (and all bubbles, in fact) abuts (or close to)
the right hand side of the bubble (currently there's quite a bit of gap), and
starts UNDER the token count / metadata in the top right corner of the bubble."

Owner addition, quoted in substance: the scrollbar must be ALWAYS VISIBLE when
the bubble's content is cut off and absent when it is not — not the platform's
hover-only overlay bar.

| call | old → new | what was decided |
|---|---|---|
| the width cap | `--agent-bubble-cap` **75% → 82.5%** | Exactly ten percent, on the one token every width in the feed derives from, so the centered tool cards, the prompt's right rail, the footer dock and the turn-ended line all widen with the response rather than drifting apart from it. |
| the length cap | `--feed-cap-lines` **25 → 27.5** | Exactly ten percent of the SAME line box each bubble already counts in, so nothing about a bubble's leading changed. The half line is kept rather than rounded to 28: 27.5 is the ruling's figure, and a budget ending mid-line leaves a sliver of the next one showing, which is itself the signal that the box scrolls. |
| where the scrollbar's GAP came from | `.bubble` padding `0.6rem 0.9rem` → `0.6rem var(--bubble-scroll-gap) 0.6rem 0.9rem`, gap = **2px** | The gap was the bubble's own right padding, sitting between the scroll box and the border. Padding ON a scroll container does not inset its bar, so the fix had to be at the container above it. |
| where the inset the bubble gave up went | new `.bubble-scroll` wrapping the existing `.bubble-body` | The scroll box is now a bare wrapper with no horizontal padding of its own (its right edge IS the bubble's inner edge) and `.bubble-body` is demoted to the CONTENT wrapper carrying `padding-right: calc(0.9rem - var(--bubble-scroll-gap))`. Every rule and test written against `.bubble-body` — the markdown resets, the flush first/last child margins, the whole-body prose repaints — is untouched. |
| the metadata strip | `.bubble` `flex-direction: row` → `column`; `.turn-meta` appended BEFORE the body | The corner stamp used to hold its own flex column beside the body, which by construction put the bar alongside the token figure for the bubble's whole height. It is now a full-width strip above the scroll box, outside it, so the bar starts under it. `.turn-meta`'s `-0.5rem` right margin is dropped with the padding it used to eat into. |
| the prompt bubble's grid | `.bubble.user { display: grid }` and the two `grid-area: 1 / 1` placements **retired** | The grid existed to OVERLAY the hover stamp on the body, which is the one arrangement the ruling forbids. The prompt keeps `.bubble`'s shared column, and its author line is the strip. Side effect, recorded deliberately: the author label now renders ABOVE the body, which is what its own comment always claimed ("the prompt's author line, above its body") — under the grid it auto-placed into row 2, below the body. |
| tool cards, shell and subagent bubbles | `.tool-card`'s scroll boxes take `margin-right: calc(var(--bubble-scroll-gap) - 0.75rem)` with the same figure back as padding | A card has a dozen non-scrolling children that all need its 0.75rem inset, so the card keeps its padding and the scroll boxes step OUT of it instead — the mirror of the bubble's arrangement, and the text does not move. The shell's tail and the subagent's sub-feed panel needed nothing beyond the bubble's own right inset dropping: their heads are already siblings above them. |
| the always-visible bar | new `::-webkit-scrollbar` / `-track` / `-thumb` rules, 8px, `var(--border)` / `var(--muted)` | Styling `::-webkit-scrollbar` at all opts WebKit out of the fading overlay bar and into a classic one, painted for as long as the box overflows and laid out in its own gutter (so content is never under it). `overflow-y` stays `auto`, never `scroll`: `auto` is what keeps a short bubble bar-free, and the suite forbids `scroll` anywhere in the sheet. No new color token. |
| what was NOT done | — | The subagent bubble's sub-feed panel still carries no line cap of its own, so it has nothing to scroll; only its POSITION was corrected. Adding a cap there is a behavior change the ruling did not order. |

## The thinking-status substatus lifecycle (2026-09-14)

Owner ruling: status `thinking` carries the substatus lifecycle
`submitting → [classifying] → working`. No `receipt` substatus — "received"
is the entry edge of `submitting` (the directive-type check between them is
synchronous and sub-millisecond), so it earns no arm.

- `submitting` — the daemon has the prompt, has acked all clients, and is
  delivering to the shim; the shim has not taken it. Set on RECEIPT, for all
  clients (footer + roster + tab-bar), before the shim call.
- `classifying` — the prompt is parked behind a running turn and the LLM judge
  is deciding auto-interrupt vs hold-for-turn-end (promptqueue `ArmClassifying`).
  A NEW footer substatus arm. Only reachable when something is ahead in the
  queue.
- `working` — the shim accepted the turn (StartTurn success); it is running.
  Renders as the text "working" (the existing thinking substatus arm).

A StartTurn failure after the receipt ack surfaces loudly (a failed/blocked
status), never a silent revert to idle.

The auto-interrupt classifier already exists end to end (classify.go: judge →
interject → KillTurn, with the footer's waiting-interrupting state).

## Keep-alive rows are no longer stored (2026-09-23)

| call | chosen | why |
|---|---|---|
| whether any purpose needs stored keep-alive rows | none does | The send, the result, the scope close and the rewind anchor are the shim's memory; `resumeSessionAt` reads the vendor's transcript; a refused rewind re-delivers the prompt it holds. The store serves only page lines and bash runs, and the daemon has no store client, so the "accounting" rows had no reader. |
| the sidecar's structural rule | the prompt marker plus the transcript's own links | A user record with a `promptId` is the keep-alive's when that promptId is a marked prompt's; a user record with none is classified by the marker when it is prose, else by its parent; every other record follows its `parentUuid`. The shim-minted client uuid was not used: the SDK declares its echo on the stream, not that the transcript's user record keeps it. |
| a compaction beside a keep-alive | served | A `compact_boundary` names no parent, the conversation really was compacted, and the shim clears its rewind anchor on it; the old one-bool rule withheld it, which disagreed with the stream plane's own attribution of the same key. |
| a restart mid keep-alive | the file prefix is classified before the first delivery (`tail.Primer`) | The boot rewind stops at the last turn start, which is another prompt whenever one landed between the keep-alive's prompt and its reply. A failed prefix read fails the poll and is retried. |
| a keep-alive turn that spawns a subagent | not addressed | Sidechain files carry no marker; this was equally uncovered by the one-bool rule and is outside the keep-alive path as the task scoped it. |
